## 2026-11-10 · Panel tables: every redraw is fitted before it is painted, not on the next frame
- The table engine's `programarFit()` (`static/platform/admin-table.mjs`) runs the fit at the end of the task that asked for it (`queueMicrotask`), no longer on the next animation frame. Every panel table goes through it: Pacientes, Mis turnos, the doctor's patients, the catalog editors (Contenido), Preparaciones, Roles, Usabilidad, the AI register, and every table the automatic sweep adopts. Several calls in one task still make one fit; `dispose()` drops a queued one.
- The automatic sweep runs inside an animation frame; a fit it requested with `requestAnimationFrame` landed one frame later, after a paint at the natural width. A microtask still runs before that paint.
- One path keeps the frame: the medium's `ResizeObserver` (`fitEnCuadro()`). It is delivered after layout; a fit inside that delivery rewraps rows, changes the observed medium's height mid-delivery and raises the browser's «ResizeObserver loop» error. It answers a change of available width, not a redraw.
- Pacientes sets its doctor cells' «+N» in the microtask after the fit (it waited two frames for the fit), so a redraw paints names and «+N» together; the observer and the late font keep the two-frame path.
- Supersedes the note of the queue entry above that «the other panel tables still fit on the next frame»; the queue keeps its direct `autoFit()`, which Q02 reads in the same task.
- Proof: `test/admin-tabla.test.mjs` pins that `programarFit()` holds a microtask and no animation frame; `test/admin-table-disposal.test.mjs` that the fit lands after the asking task with no frame requested and never after dispose; `test/e2e/admin-patients-lifecycle.spec.mjs` (1280) redraws Pacientes with two keyboard sorts and reads `table-layout: fixed` one microtask later — red on the frame-deferred engine.
- Not covered: at 768 a late web font can still rewrap the queue's button row (one CLS 0.25 with an artificial 250 ms font delay). Below 1024 no widths are fitted, so this is font metrics, not the fit; the fix is a metric-matched fallback face (`size-adjust`) in the theme fonts, a separate change.
Why: the queue's CLS in Q02 (0.1065 on a slower Linux browser) came from a fit deferred to the next frame; the same deferral sat in the shared engine under every other panel table.
Undo: revert the commit.
Source: `static/platform/admin-table.mjs`, `static/platform/admin-patients.mjs`; tests `test/admin-tabla.test.mjs`, `test/admin-table-disposal.test.mjs`, `test/e2e/admin-patients-lifecycle.spec.mjs`.

## 2026-11-10 · Swallowed failures after a saved action report closed codes; a failed day-before reminder is retried
Decided: the steps that run after an action has ALREADY persisted, and that swallowed any error without a trace, now write one log line (the request id and the exception's type, never its text nor the patient) and report a closed launch-signal code (`core/launch_signals.FAILURE_CODES`, nine new codes; `handled(code)` as the existing fourteen call sites do). Patient notices in `notifications/send.py`: `notify_received_failed`, `notify_approved_failed` (email or WhatsApp), `notify_rejected_failed` (email or the remedy's WhatsApp), `notify_cancelled_failed`; the inner WhatsApp helpers of the confirmation and the rejection report the same codes. The 503's existing warning line is unchanged (no request id). A new request's side steps in `modules/turnos/booking_create.py`: `booking_referral_failed` (the referral file was not stored, or not linked to the request), `booking_ai_save_failed` (the referral reading was not saved; `DbUnavailable` still propagates as before), `booking_hold_link_failed` (the slot hold was not tied to the request). The 503 «database unavailable» of `core/deps.py` (`DbUnavailable`, `QueryCanceled`, `LockNotAvailable` that no route caught): `db_unavailable`, at most once per 15 minutes per process, gated before `handled` so the hourly counter is not written on every refused request, and reported from its own thread because the counter write goes to the very database that is failing; the answer (503, body, `Retry-After: 3`) is unchanged. The day-before reminder was claimed before the send and a failed send lost it for good: `notify_reminder` now answers False when the reminder could not be built or every channel that was tried refused it, and `reminders.tick` then gives the claim back (`db_booking.release_reminder`, `reminder_at` to NULL) and reports `reminder_failed`; the next sweep tries again. One channel accepted is a delivered reminder (a retry would repeat it); nothing to send (no address, no phone) or a channel off by configuration (`mail.unsendable`: mail off, the outbound kill switch armed, links pointing at this machine, no provider routed, or the provider without its keys; WhatsApp with notices off, no provider or the kill switch) keeps the claim, so such a row is not picked up every sweep (a reminder blocked by missing keys stays claimed even if the keys arrive later that day). A release that cannot be written (the database is down) loses that reminder, as a crash between claim and send always did. There is no attempt counter: the retries are bounded by the sweep's own window — a row is a candidate only the day before its appointment, 09:00–21:00, and each sweep reads its rows once, so one try per sweep (with one scheduler and the default 30-minute `TURNOS_REMINDER_INTERVAL_MIN`, about 24 at most; more sweeping processes or a shorter interval mean more), each logged and counted, with at most one Sentry event per code per 15 minutes per process. Behaviour for people is otherwise unchanged: no notice or side step breaks the request that triggered it. Nothing personal leaves: the event message is the code alone (`test_launch_signals.TestNewCodesLeaveAsCodes`). The failing test came first, red on `ddafa07d`: `test_reminders.TestSendFailureReleasesTheClaim`.
Why: the swallowed-failures review of 2026-11-10 (top 10: notices, the reminder claim, the request's side steps and the 503 left no trace and could not reach Sentry); the product owner's hub, 2026-11-10: «handled calls with new FAILURE_CODES at notifications/send.py, booking_create.py and core/deps.py, the 503 one rate-limited to one event per 15 min. The day-before reminder claimed before send is a real loss, please make it a failing test plus fix: release the claim on send failure.»
Rejected: an attempt counter column for reminders (a schema change for what the sweep window already bounds); one shared `notify_failed` code (the kind of notice is the first thing to know, and it costs no personal data).
Undo: revert the commit (no migration).
By: hospital agent, agreed with the product owner's hub 2026-11-10
Source: `core/launch_signals.py`, `notifications/send.py`, `notifications/reminders.py`, `notifications/mail.py`, `core/db_booking.py`, `modules/turnos/booking_create.py`, `core/deps.py`, `docs/architecture/deploy.md`; tests `test/test_reminders.py`, `test/test_launch_signals.py`.

## 2026-11-10 · A failed pre-consult interview invitation is retried, like the day-before reminder
Decided: the second day-before sweep (`reminders.tick_interview`, column `interview_reminder_at`) claimed its row before the send and marked it whatever happened, so a provider refusal lost the invitation for good. Now `notify_interview_reminder` answers False when the invitation could not be built or the provider refused it, the intake hook (`modules/intake/app.py`) hands that answer back through `app_modules.hooks.call`, and the sweep gives the claim back (`db_booking.release_reminder(sid, "interview_reminder_at")`) and reports the closed code `interview_reminder_failed`; the next sweep tries again. The claim stays when the invitation went out, when there is nothing to send (the interview is done, intake is off for the doctor, no address) and when mail is off by configuration (`mail.unsendable`, as for the appointment reminder). A hook that raises is still swallowed by `hooks.call` and counted as processed (the notice itself never raises). Retries are bounded the same way: the day before, 09:00–21:00, one try per sweep. The failing test came first, red on `8c4ef249`: `test_reminders.TestInterviewSendFailureReleasesTheClaim`.
Why: the product owner's hub, 2026-11-10: tick_interview — the same fix, a separate commit on the same branch.
Undo: revert the commit (no migration).
By: hospital agent, agreed with the product owner's hub 2026-11-10
Source: `notifications/send.py` (`notify_interview_reminder`), `notifications/reminders.py` (`tick_interview`, `_release`), `modules/intake/app.py`, `core/db_booking.py` (`release_reminder`), `core/launch_signals.py`; tests `test/test_reminders.py`, `test/test_launch_signals.py`.

## 2026-11-10 · A reminder that keeps failing stops after three tries; one mail send answers why; every listed 503 reports
Decided: three follow-ups to the two entries above, after the product owner's hub reviewed the branch. (1) The day-before reminder and the pre-consult interview invitation are tried at most three times per row in one process (`reminders._MAX_TRIES`, counted per column and request for the day being swept, the map emptied when the day changes): the first two failed tries give the claim back as before, the third keeps it, so the row is no longer a candidate, and nothing more is reported for it. Every failed try still logs one line and reports its code (`reminder_failed`, `interview_reminder_failed`), at most three per row. A restart starts the count again. This replaces «the retries are bounded by the sweep's own window», which was wrong: a permanent refusal (a hard bounce, a 4xx) came back every sweep of the day, up to about 24 times per row, each with a `mail_failed` and a `reminder_failed`. (2) `notifications.mail.deliver` is the one email send and answers why: `sent`, `skipped` (configuration kept it from leaving: links to this machine, no provider routed, `MAIL_ENABLED=0`, the outbound kill switch armed, the provider without its keys) or `failed` (the provider refused it or the send raised; also when the reason cannot be told, so a reminder is retried, never lost). The provider is routed and asked once; a refusal is read for its reason afterwards from that same provider. `mail.send` is `deliver(...) == "sent"`, so every other sender and its counters behave as before. `mail.unsendable`, which repeated those conditions and routed the provider a second time, is gone; the day-before reminders read `deliver`'s answer. (3) The routes that answer their own 503 for `db.DbUnavailable` named in the review report it too, through the same 15-minute window per process as the uncaught 503 (`deps.signal_db_unavailable`, public now): admin content, patients («who saw this patient» and the data export), reports, AI costs, and the doctor's own workspace (profile, its save, onboarding). `admin_panel/admin_users.py` answers no 503 (it degrades to the four built-in roles), so it has nothing to report. Other routes that catch `DbUnavailable` and answer their own 503 do not report it yet; `docs/architecture/deploy.md` says so. Behaviour for people is unchanged. The failing tests came first, red on `87a2a714`: `test_reminders` `test_a_reminder_the_provider_keeps_refusing_stops_after_three_tries` and `test_an_invitation_the_provider_keeps_refusing_stops_after_three_tries` (5 sends instead of 3).
Why: the product owner's hub review of the branch, 2026-11-10: «at constant mail refusal, hard bounce or 4xx, the reminder repeats every sweep, up to ~24 times a day per row, with mail_failed and reminder_failed each time; the "bounded" comment is wrong. We propose a per-sid attempt cap in the process, 3-4, or a retry deadline»; «db_unavailable is caught only by the general 503 handler, while admin_content_router, admin_patients_router, reports_router, ai_costs_router, admin_users, doctor_workspace_screen give their own 503s — either fix the wording in the comment and deploy.md, or call _signal_db_unavailable from there too»; «unsendable repeats send's conditions and calls transport a second time, they can diverge; better let send return the reason of the refusal».
Rejected: a retry deadline instead of a count (one more clock to reason about; the count says plainly how often a row is tried); an attempts column in the database (a schema change for a cap that a process can keep); `send` itself returning the reason (its answer goes into JSON as `email_enviado` and is compared as a bool by every other sender, so a richer value would change them); wiring every route that answers its own 503 in this change (about twenty more files outside the review's list).
Undo: revert the commit (no migration).
By: hospital agent, after the product owner's hub review 2026-11-10
Source: `notifications/reminders.py` (`_MAX_TRIES`, `_failed_try`, `_release`), `notifications/mail.py` (`deliver`, `_refusal`, `send`), `notifications/send.py`, `core/deps.py` (`signal_db_unavailable`), `admin_panel/admin_content_router.py`, `admin_panel/admin_patients_router.py`, `admin_panel/reports_router.py`, `ai/ai_costs_router.py`, `admin_panel/doctor_workspace_screen.py`, `docs/architecture/deploy.md`, `docs/architecture/notificaciones-y-lista-espera.md`; tests `test/test_reminders.py`, `test/test_launch_signals.py`, `test/test_booking_requests.py`.

## 2026-11-10 · Every 503 of a database that does not answer goes through one function, which sends the signal
Decided: `core.deps.answer_db_unavailable` is the one answer for a database that does not answer: it reports `db_unavailable` through the shared 15-minute window per process (`deps.signal_db_unavailable`, now called only from there) and answers 503 in the shape the caller always sent — by default the JSON of `core.http_json` (`deps.DB_UNAVAILABLE`, or the caller's `body`), the gzip variant when the caller passes its `request`, and `build(status)` for any other shape (an HTML page, an accounts `Result`, an `AuthResult`, a domain `(status, body)`, an `HTTPException` to raise). Every route that caught `db.DbUnavailable` and answered its own 503 now answers through it, and so do the domain results that carried a 503 for the database and the handler for the `DbUnavailable` no route caught: prompts, calendar feed, the doctor's appointments, panel settings, the pre-consult interview, intake admin and consults, accounts and patient login, legal texts, doctor photos, chart summary, AI register, agenda print, preferences, the doctor's patients, the appointment offer, the study routes, chat voice and MCP, passkeys, staff login and logout, password reset, the access-log refusal, the brand color, plus the five routes of the follow-up above (admin content, patients, reports, AI costs, the doctor's own workspace), whose own helpers are gone. Status, headers and body of every answer are unchanged; what changes is that each of them now reports. Where the route caught any exception as «base de datos no disponible» (chat voice, MCP, the brand color, logout, the passkey counter), that 503 reports `db_unavailable` too, as its body already said. `test/test_db_unavailable_answer.py` is the guard: the number 503 (a literal, `HTTPStatus.SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE`, an `HTTP_503_…` name) may appear in the server code only inside that function and in a listed exception that is not about the database (the /ready probes of the app and the worker, dictation switched off or unconfigured, the translation provider, no AI configured, missing PDF libraries, the demo person not seeded, an unreadable catalog file, a misconfigured patient portal, the identity provider, and the statuses that AI providers, S3 and B2 answer us); a listed one that disappears is red too, and so is a call to `signal_db_unavailable` outside `core/deps.py`.
Why: the product owner's hub, 2026-11-10, on the routes that answered their own 503 without the signal: «yes, wire them, in a separate branch — but not as a copy of the call in 25 files: better one shared 503 answer for DbUnavailable in core/deps that sends the signal itself, and the routers call it instead of their own 503; then a new router will not forget the signal. Plus an inventory test: no 503 returned past this function. The branch goes after silent-signals and the follow-up.»
Rejected: a decorator or middleware that turns a caught `DbUnavailable` into the signal (the routes catch it to send their own body, so it never reaches a middleware); one fixed JSON body for every route (the bodies differ — translated texts, `base_no_disponible`, HTML pages — and clients read them); a text search for `status=503` instead of the AST (misses a tuple `return 503, {...}`, an `HTTPException(503)` and the `HTTPStatus` names).
Undo: revert the commit (no migration).
By: hospital agent, agreed with the product owner's hub 2026-11-10
Source: `core/deps.py` (`answer_db_unavailable`, `DB_UNAVAILABLE`, `render_db_unavailable`), the routers and domain modules named above, `docs/architecture/deploy.md`; tests `test/test_db_unavailable_answer.py`, `test/test_launch_signals.py`.

## 2026-11-10 · db_unavailable means the database does not answer: the broad catches are narrowed
Decided: the six places that caught any exception and answered «base de datos no disponible» now answer that 503, through `deps.answer_db_unavailable` and its `db_unavailable` signal, only for a database that does not answer — `deps.DB_UNAVAILABLE_ERRORS`, the same three the uncaught handler counts (`db.DbUnavailable`, psycopg's `QueryCanceled` and `LockNotAvailable`). Any other failure there logs its exception type (`log_pii.exc`, never the message) and answers its own error with a 500, without the signal: the chat dictation model in the panel and the brand colour answer `admin.srv.error.no_guardado` («no se pudo guardar; probá de nuevo», English «could not save; try again»); the MCP tool call with a token that could not be read answers JSON-RPC -32603 «error interno del servidor de turnos», and the patient's own MCP token in Mi cuenta answers the same text (the wording the MCP tool errors already used); the staff logout answers `cierre_no_registrado` (the cookie is cleared all the same, `revoked: false`); a passkey sign-in whose counter could not be advanced answers `contador_no_guardado` and still issues no session. The screens show their existing generic phrase for an unknown code («No se pudo usar la passkey.», «No se pudo iniciar sesión.»). No other place answered «database unavailable» from a broad catch; the MCP tool-call wrapper already told `DbUnavailable` apart. A JSON string or number where the panel sends an object (chat voice model, brand colour) is neither: it stays outside those catches and answers the body family's 400 «se esperaba un objeto» through `api_body.call`, never the new 500 (`test_no_body_crashes_a_route`; the four cases of `test/golden/body_contract_admin_settings.json` move from the old false 503 to that 400).
Why: the product owner's hub, 2026-11-10: «narrow it to DbUnavailable. The db_unavailable signal must mean only that the database is unavailable, otherwise a false alarm and the counter lies. Other exceptions in these places — chat voice, MCP, brand colour, logout, passkey counter — should log the exception type and answer their own error, without db_unavailable; if the answer to the user there now says "database unavailable" while the cause is another, fix that to an honest text too.»
Rejected: 502 for these failures (nothing upstream answered badly: the failure is ours); a new patient-facing text for the MCP token (the account screen does not show the body of a refusal, and the MCP texts are one register for the AI client).
Undo: revert the commit (no migration).
By: hospital agent, agreed with the product owner's hub 2026-11-10
Source: `core/deps.py` (`DB_UNAVAILABLE_ERRORS`), `modules/chat/voice_router.py`, `modules/chat/mcp_api.py` (`_INTERNAL_ERROR`, `_internal_error`), `patient_web/branding.py`, `auth/admin_auth.py` (`logout_result`), `auth/passkeys.py` (`login_result`), `locales/es/admin.srv.json`, `locales/en/admin.srv.json`; tests `test/test_db_unavailable_answer.py` (`TestOnlyTheDatabaseSignals`).

## 2026-11-10 · A database that stops answering under a statement is unavailable too; a deadlock is not
Decided: psycopg's `OperationalError` that means the database stopped answering is a `DbUnavailable`: the pooled `with` of `db._connect()` (`_PooledConn.__exit__`, the exit every statement of the app leaves through, where the web pool's limits already became `DbTimeout`) turns it into `db.DbConnectionLost`, both when a statement raises it and when the commit does. Which ones: an `OperationalError` with no SQLSTATE (the client lost the server: a closed socket, a dropped network) and the SQLSTATE classes 08 (connection exception), 53 (insufficient resources: too many connections, disk full, out of memory), 57 (operator intervention: `AdminShutdown`, `CrashShutdown`, `CannotConnectNow`, `DatabaseDropped`), 58 (system error) and F0 (configuration file) — `db._LOST_CLASSES`. Every other `OperationalError` is the database answering about the statement and stays what it is, explicitly excluded: 40 (`TransactionRollback`: deadlock, serialization failure), 54 (program limit), 55 (object state; its `LockNotAvailable` is still the lock-timeout `DbTimeout`), 2F/38/39 (routines), HV (foreign data). So the six narrowed places (chat voice model, brand colour, MCP tool-call token, MCP account token, staff logout, passkey counter) answer a Postgres restart or a dropped connection with the 503 and `db_unavailable` again, a deadlock with their own 500 and no signal; the uncaught handler answers `DbConnectionLost` as any `DbUnavailable`. `db.UNAVAILABLE_ERRORS` is the one tuple (`deps.DB_UNAVAILABLE_ERRORS` is it): `DbUnavailable` plus psycopg's `QueryCanceled` and `LockNotAvailable` for a limit hit outside a pooled block. The message of `DbConnectionLost` names psycopg's error type only.
Why: the product owner's hub review of TASK-75, 2026-11-10: a Postgres restart or a broken connection in the middle of a request raises `psycopg.OperationalError` / `AdminShutdown` / `CannotConnectNow`, `_PooledConn.__exit__` converted only the timeouts, so the six narrowed places answered 500 without the signal where they used to answer 503 with it; «add psycopg.OperationalError; its subclasses where the database answered — exclude explicitly or accept, your choice, record it».
Rejected: adding `psycopg.OperationalError` itself to the tuple (Python's `except` cannot leave out its subclasses, so a deadlock anywhere would be a false `db_unavailable`); accepting the deadlocks and serialization failures (the database answered; the signal would lie, as the product owner's hub asked it never to); a list of psycopg classes instead of SQLSTATE classes (psycopg's error classes are flat under `OperationalError`, the SQLSTATE class is what groups them).
Undo: revert the commit (no migration).
By: hospital agent, agreed with the product owner's hub 2026-11-10
Source: `core/db.py` (`DbConnectionLost`, `_LOST_CLASSES`, `_unavailable_error`, `UNAVAILABLE_ERRORS`, `_PooledConn.__exit__`), `core/deps.py` (`DB_UNAVAILABLE_ERRORS`); tests `test/test_db_unavailable_answer.py` (`TestTheConnectionExit`, `TestOnlyTheDatabaseSignals`).

## 2026-11-10 · The access log refuses as «database unavailable» only when the database does not answer
Decided: `access_log.record` and `record_many` still refuse the read whenever the access row cannot be written, but they raise `AccessLogUnavailable` (a `DbUnavailable`: 503 and `db_unavailable`) only for `db.UNAVAILABLE_ERRORS`; any other failure of the insert raises `AccessLogFailed`, a plain exception with the error type alone as its message, which no route answers as 503 — the request ends in the server's own error and the read does not happen.
Why: the product owner's hub review of TASK-75, 2026-11-10: `record`/`record_many` wrapped ANY exception into `AccessLogUnavailable`, so an insert failing for a reason that has nothing to do with availability reported `db_unavailable` too.
Rejected: letting the original exception through (its text may carry a row's values into the traceback in the log).
Undo: revert the commit (no migration).
By: hospital agent, agreed with the product owner's hub 2026-11-10
Source: `core/access_log.py` (`AccessLogFailed`, `_refusal`), `docs/architecture/registro-de-accesos.md`; tests `test/test_db_unavailable_answer.py` (`TestTheAccessLogRefusal`).

## 2026-11-10 · A failed reminder is retried later and later, and one that never went out is not counted as sent
Decided: replaces the three tries per row of the 2026-11-10 follow-up. A day-before reminder or pre-consult invitation that did not go out is tried again on a growing wait in this process: 30 minutes after the first failed try, then 1 h, 2 h, 4 h; when the next try would come after the sweep window closes (21:00 of the day before) the row is given up for the day. With the default 30-minute sweep a permanent refusal (a hard bounce, a 4xx) is tried five times (10:00, 10:30, 11:30, 13:30, 17:30 for a first try at 10:00), each with its `reminder_failed` / `interview_reminder_failed`, and a provider down for an hour and a half no longer burns tomorrow's reminders: they go out at the next try. Every failed try gives its claim back, the last one too, so a reminder that never went out keeps `reminder_at` empty and the report's `recordatorios_enviados` (and the «ahorro» built on it) does not count it; the process remembers the rows it gave up (`_tries`, per column and request, for the day being swept) and does not claim them again. The waits are counted from the time of the failed try, and a sweep up to five minutes early still tries (`_EARLY`), so the jitter of the sweep does not push a try to the next one. A restart starts the waits again from the first try; the state is per process, as the three tries were (one sweeping worker per installation). Two cases still keep the column filled and are counted, as before: a release that cannot be written (the database is down: that one reminder is lost, as a crash between the claim and the send always was), and a reminder with nothing to send (no address and no phone, or mail off by configuration), which is not a failure and is not retried.
Why: the product owner's hub review of TASK-75, 2026-11-10: «`_MAX_TRIES = 3` counts sweeps, a sweep runs every 30 minutes — a mail provider outage of about 1.5 hours burns all of tomorrow's reminders. Count by time or only the non-transient refusals — your choice»; and «the abandoned claim keeps `reminder_at` filled, while `core/db_reports.py` counts `reminder_at IS NOT NULL` as sent — it inflates the «ahorro» in the hospital's report. Exclude such rows (or another honest way)».
Rejected: counting only non-transient refusals (the providers' answers are not classified today: `mail.deliver` answers sent / skipped / failed); a new column for a given-up reminder (a schema change for what the empty column already says); keeping the claim on the last try and excluding it in the report (nothing in the row tells it from a sent one).
Undo: revert the commit (no migration).
By: hospital agent, agreed with the product owner's hub 2026-11-10
Source: `notifications/reminders.py` (`_RETRY_FIRST`, `_waiting`, `_failed_try`, `_release`), `core/db_reports.py` (`reports_funnel`), `docs/architecture/deploy.md`; tests `test/test_reminders.py` (`TestSendFailureReleasesTheClaim`, `TestInterviewSendFailureReleasesTheClaim`).

## 2026-11-10 · A reminder with nothing to send is not counted as sent
Decided: `notify_reminder` answers one of `mail.deliver`'s three words instead of a bool: `sent` (a channel accepted it), `failed` (it could not be built, or every channel tried refused it — the growing retries of the entry above) and `skipped` (nothing to send: no address and no phone, or the channels off by configuration, the outbound kill switch included). A `skipped` reminder keeps its claim (`reminder_at`), so no sweep builds it again — in this process or after a restart — and is marked in a new column, `booking_requests.reminder_skipped` (Alembic `0041_booking_reminder_skipped`, boolean, default false, no backfill: older claimed rows cannot be told apart and stay counted); `core/db_reports.reports_funnel` counts as sent only `reminder_at IS NOT NULL AND NOT reminder_skipped`, so neither `recordatorios_enviados` nor the «ahorro» built on it counts it. It is no failure: no signal, no retry. `tick` returns how many reminders went out (journey j22's first sweep expects 2, not 3: C switched every channel off). This replaces the last case of the entry above («a reminder with nothing to send … is counted»). A mark that cannot be written (the database is down after the claim) leaves it counted, as a crash between the claim and the send would. The pre-consult interview invitation is unchanged (no report counts it).
Why: the product owner's hub, 2026-11-10, on the hospital agent's question: «option 2 — in this same series, a separate commit on top of 7345ace5. A reminder with nowhere to go (no email and no phone, mail off by configuration) is not counted as sent and does not enter the «ahorro»: notify_reminder says «nothing to send» apart from «sent», the report does not count such rows; how to store the mark — your choice».
Rejected: giving the claim back and remembering the row in the process (tried first: a restart builds every such row again, and under the outbound kill switch the queue proof `test_business_queue_proof` loses its durable claim); keeping the claim with nothing to tell the rows apart; `None` for «nothing to send» (a falsy answer read as a success or a failure depending on the caller).
Undo: revert the code and keep `alembic/versions/0041_booking_reminder_skipped.py` (an unused column with a default harms nothing; a revision is never removed).
By: hospital agent, agreed with the product owner's hub 2026-11-10
Source: `notifications/send.py` (`notify_reminder`), `notifications/reminders.py` (`tick`, `_nothing_to_send`), `core/db_booking.py` (`mark_reminder_skipped`), `core/db_reports.py` (`reports_funnel`), `core/schema_model.py`, `alembic/versions/0041_booking_reminder_skipped.py`, `alembic/schema-head.txt`, `docs/architecture/notificaciones-y-lista-espera.md`, `docs/architecture/deploy.md`; tests `test/test_reminders.py` (`test_what_the_reminder_answers`, `test_nothing_to_send_is_not_counted_as_sent`), `test/test_notifications.py`, `test/test_launch_signals.py`, `test/test_alembic_revisions.py` (`TestBookingReminderSkippedRevision`), `test/e2e/journey-j22-notification-channels.spec.mjs`.

## 2026-11-11 · The pooled exit: a protocol violation is ours, a broken commit does not know what it wrote, a failed rollback keeps the body's error
Decided: three changes to `_PooledConn.__exit__` and what it raises. (1) SQLSTATE 08P01 (protocol violation) is left out of `_LOST_CLASSES`'s class 08 (`_NOT_LOST_STATES`): it is the client sending what the protocol does not allow, a bug of ours, so it stays psycopg's own error — the route's 500, no `db_unavailable`. (2) A connection lost under the commit itself (no body error, `conn.__exit__` raising one of the lost classes) is `db.DbCommitUnknown`, a `DbConnectionLost`: still a database that does not answer, so 503 and the signal, every route unchanged in status; but the write may have landed, so the answer does not promise that asking again is safe. `deps.answer_db_unavailable`'s default JSON for it is `{"error": "<admin.srv.error.db_resultado_desconocido>", "outcome": "unknown"}` (Spanish «la base de datos dejó de responder al confirmar; no sabemos si se guardó: revisá antes de repetir», English in `locales/en`, the other eight languages by the product owner's hub), told from the exception the caller is handling (`exc=`, by default `sys.exc_info()`); the handler for the one no route caught answers the same body without `Retry-After`. A caller that passes its own `body` or `build` keeps its shape (their text still says «no disponible»: changing ~70 answers is not part of this). A commit the database refused (a serialization failure, a deferred constraint) is known not to have written and stays what it is; a limit on the commit stays `DbTimeout`. (3) When the body raised and the rollback that follows fails too, the body's error goes on (as `DbConnectionLost` from it when it was a lost connection) and the rollback's is attached to it as a note, `rollback failed too: <type>` — the log shows why the request failed. psycopg 3.3 already swallows its own rollback errors with a warning; this holds the same for whatever the pooled exit wraps.
Why: the product owner's hub review of TASK-75, 2026-11-11, optional notes on the signals series: 08P01 went out as «database unavailable» with the signal; a commit that broke answered a retryable 503 although a retry may double the effect («do not promise a safe retry — its own status/message "resultado desconocido" — or at least record the decision; your choice»); a rollback exception overwrote the body's.
Rejected: a status other than 503 for the unknown commit (the database really does not answer; the signal, the 503 inventory of `test_db_unavailable_answer.py` and every route's shape stay one contract); only recording the decision (a human who reads «no disponible» simply repeats the booking); setting the rollback error as the body error's `__context__` (that slot may already hold the body's own cause chain).
Undo: revert the commit (no migration); the two locale keys can stay.
By: hospital agent
Source: `core/db.py` (`DbCommitUnknown`, `_NOT_LOST_STATES`, `_unavailable_error`, `_PooledConn.__exit__`), `core/deps.py` (`_commit_unknown`, `answer_db_unavailable`, `_uncaught_db_unavailable`, `render_db_unavailable`), `locales/es/admin.srv.json`, `locales/en/admin.srv.json`, `docs/architecture/deploy.md`; tests `test/test_db_unavailable_answer.py` (`TestTheConnectionExit`, `TestTheAnswers`, `TestOnlyTheDatabaseSignals.test_chat_voice_model`), `test/test_db_domain_modules.py` (`CONNECTION_API`).

## 2026-11-11 · Public catalog lists: the page keeps its height while the list module and its data arrive
- `listing.mjs` empties the server's slot on mount and, when the list has audience/sex filters (/doctors, the studies table), draws only after `/api/account/me` answers. The new empty slot lost the server's height reserve (`--lst-reserva*` lived in the old slot's `style`), so for that whole wait the page collapsed to the viewport and the footer jumped into view and back: CLS 0.26 at 1280 and 0.37 at 390 on the demo, with or without the web fonts. The new slot now copies the old slot's `style`.
- The centers mosaic reserved 168px per desktop row of three for a ~430px row, and nothing for two columns: the footer was in view at 1280 and left it when `tiles.mjs` drew (CLS 0.18). The server now gives only counts (`--tiles-n`, `--tiles-filas-2`, `--tiles-filas-3`) and the shell CSS turns them into a height with the grid's own breakpoints (2 columns from 664px, 3 from 980px): rows × (photo width × its aspect ratio + text and gap, 145-170px measured on the demo), so the reserve follows the viewport instead of being right at one width. `[data-tiles]` carries the grid's 14px top margin from the first paint.
- The desktop breakout of a catalog list (`.wrap>[data-listado]:not(.lst-in-column)` and its footer) is also in the shell's inline CSS: it used to apply only when `listing.mjs` injected its style, so the block widened sideways after the first paint (CLS 0.02 on a center page, 0.04-0.05 on /doctors and an area page at 1280). `listing.mjs` keeps the same rules for client-side mounts; `test/listing-breakout-parity.test.mjs` holds the two copies equal.
- There is no /services page: the services lists live on area pages (/d/<area>) and center pages (/center/<slug>); both are in the test.
- Proof: `test/e2e/catalog-listing-layout-shift.spec.mjs` holds the list/tiles module and every /api/ response and asserts no layout shift while the page sits on the server markup, after the module mounts with the data held, and after the data lands, at 390, 640, 820 and 1280 on /doctors, /centers, a center page and an area page (red on the previous code: 0.20/0.13, 0.01/0.18, 0/0.02, 0/0.05).
Why: pinamar-turnos 2026-11-10 (d2329990), a side finding of the web-font fallback slice: /doctors moved on load at every width, fonts or not.
Undo: revert the commit.
Source: `static/platform/listing.mjs`, `patient_web/site_shell.py`, `patient_web/site_page_centers.py`; test `test/e2e/catalog-listing-layout-shift.spec.mjs`.

## 2026-11-10 · Theme fonts: a metric-matched fallback face paints until the web font lands, and the panel tables refit when it does
- Each theme names a "<family> Fallback" face after its web font: `Manrope Fallback` and `IBM Plex Mono Fallback` (`static/fonts/humanrounds.css`), `Fira Sans Fallback` and `Ubuntu Mono Fallback` (`static/fonts/fira.css`). They are `local()` Arial / Arimo / Liberation Sans (Courier New / Cousine / Liberation Mono for the mono faces), one face per weight and style the theme uses, with `size-adjust`, `ascent-override`, `descent-override` and `line-gap-override: 0%` measured against the web font. `tokens.css` puts the face right after the web font in `--font` and `--font-mono`. Before, the stack fell to `system-ui`: San Francisco on a Mac, Segoe UI on Windows, DejaVu Sans on Linux (about 6% wider), so the swap moved text by a different amount per machine.
- `size-adjust` is the mean width ratio over the strings the panel shows (queue rows, buttons, headers, per weight), not a percentile: per string the web font is up to 2-4% wider (digits, capitals; Fira more than Manrope). Ascent and descent are the web font's metrics divided by `size-adjust`, so line height does not move.
- Each fallback face carries the `unicode-range` union of its web font's cuts, so glyphs the web font never covers (Cyrillic in Ubuntu Mono, Greek outside Manrope) still fall to the system face instead of a scaled Arial or Courier New for good.
- The table engine refits every live table when a font face lands (`static/platform/admin-table.mjs`: one module-level listener, each face's `loaded` from the `loading` event plus `loadingdone`, through `programarFit()`; a disposed table has left `INSTANCIAS`). Per face, not only `loadingdone`: that waits for the whole set, and a slow mono or latin-ext cut would hold it. At 1280 the queue has no spare width: the doctor column is fitted to the fallback text to the pixel (170px), and «Ginecología y Obstetricia» in Manrope is about 2px wider, so the cell rewrapped for one frame (rows 62 to 80 px and back, CLS about 0.05-0.08). The refit lands before that frame paints.
- Rejected: scaling `size-adjust` up 1-2% so the fallback is never narrower. +1% fixed humanrounds at 1280 but not pinamar (CLS 0.024); +2% made Pinamar's queue button row at 768 wrap to two rows with the font blocked, the very defect this change fixes. Rejected: a widening factor in the engine while `document.fonts.status` is loading; at 1280 there is no free width to give, the column stays at 170px either way.
- Measured with a 1.5 s late font on the demo (CLS, before / after): queue 768 0.0001 / 0.00005 and 1280 0.016 / no cell changes height; with a Linux-like wide fallback before the change the queue at 390 was 0.26, the home page at 390 0.21, Médicos and Servicios at 1280 0.087, all near 0 now. Other places the face steadies: the panel nav switching between full and compact near 1280 (`static/platform/nav.mjs`), the doctor editor tabs at 360 (CLS 0.16 on Linux), the 2FA banner of the consultation lifecycle (CI Linux 0.17-0.46; its spec pins Arial), mono data (Ubuntu Mono is about 20% narrower than `ui-monospace`).
- Not the font: `/doctors` shifts 0.26-0.40 at every width while its list loads, the same with or without the face. Left for a separate change.
- Closes the «Not covered» point of the entry above.
- The queue's control row at 768 (humanrounds) had no slack at all: with the font it ended exactly at the content edge, so a day name 4px wider («Mié 11 nov» against «Mar 10 nov») wrapped the action icons even in the fallback face that measures within 1%. The search now takes `flex:1 1 200px` capped at 240px (`static/platform/admin-queue.css`): the row breaks on the 200px basis and the search grows back to 240 where there is room, so the wide layout is unchanged and up to 40px of wider text shrinks the search instead of wrapping. The phone keeps its own rule.
- Proof: `test/e2e/admin-font-fallback.spec.mjs` (both themes): with every `.woff2` aborted, the queue at 768 keeps its control rows, height within 2px and text width within 2% of the web font; with the font held back and released, CLS under 0.02 at 768 and at 1280 (red at 1280 without the refit). `test/admin-table-disposal.test.mjs` pins the refit on one face landing and on `loadingdone`, and none after dispose. `test/e2e/admin-consultation-lifecycle.spec.mjs` pins `'Manrope','Manrope Fallback'` instead of plain Arial for its font-settlement cases.
- Accepted: Safari (WebKit) applies `size-adjust` but not the ascent/descent overrides, so there the fallback keeps Arial's scaled line metrics; before it was `system-ui`, so no regression. A later font face (weight 800, a latin-ext cut) refits the tables again mid-session; that is what the wider text needs, and widths a user dragged are kept.
Why: pinamar-turnos 2026-11-10 (d2325753): a late web font rewrapped the queue's button row at 768.
Undo: revert the commit; the stack falls back to `system-ui` again.
Source: `static/fonts/humanrounds.css`, `static/fonts/fira.css`, `tokens.css`, `static/platform/admin-table.mjs`; tests `test/e2e/admin-font-fallback.spec.mjs`, `test/admin-table-disposal.test.mjs`.

## 2026-11-11 · A save of unknown outcome: short wording, and the patient gets «Mis turnos» to check
- `admin.srv.error.db_resultado_desconocido` said «al confirmar», which reads as confirming the appointment; it now says «al guardar» and fits in two lines at 390 without «;» or «:»: «la base no respondió al guardar, revisá si quedó antes de repetir» (English «the database did not answer while saving, check before trying again»). `admin.srv.error.no_guardado` loses its «;» the same way («no se pudo guardar, probá de nuevo»). The eight generated languages come from `scripts/translate_locales.py`.
- On a patient route (`deps._PATIENT_PATHS`: `/api/requests`, `/api/offer`, `/api/confirm`, `/api/appointment`, `/appointment`) the default body for `db.DbCommitUnknown` is the patient's sentence and the way to check, in the booking limit's shape: `{"error", "detalle": "No sabemos si quedó guardado. Revisá antes de repetir.", "outcome": "unknown", "accion": {"texto": "Ver mis turnos", "url": "/my-requests"}}` (new key `admin.srv.error.db_resultado_desconocido_paciente`; the link text reuses `service.success.view_requests`; `/my-requests` is where the offer and confirmation pages already send the patient, and it does not need the account page's sign-in). Sign-in and sign-up (`/api/account`) are not about an appointment and keep the neutral sentence, as do staff routes. The uncaught handler tells them apart by the same request path.
- The booking router (`booking_requests_router._db_unavailable_response`) passed its own «base de datos no disponible», which promises a safe retry, even for a broken commit — the very case where the patient's booking may have landed. For `DbCommitUnknown` it now answers the default body above; a database that simply does not answer keeps «no disponible». This narrows the earlier «a caller's own body keeps its shape» for that one router only.
- The booking wizard (`service.mjs`) renders `detalle` + the `accion` link for `outcome: "unknown"` exactly as for the booking limit; the waitlist offer page (`offer.mjs`) and the reminder's confirmation page (`confirm.mjs`) show the sentence and the link instead of «probá de nuevo». In all three the buttons stay off after an unknown outcome: a second send could duplicate or read «ya no está disponible»; the check is «Mis turnos».
Why: pinamar-turnos 2026-11-11 (d2336574), findings of the code review of the translations slice: wording read as «confirming the appointment», the patient got an error instead of the next step, and the text took 3+ lines on a phone.
Undo: revert the commit.
Source: `core/deps.py` (`_PATIENT_PATHS`, `_patient_request`, `_commit_unknown`), `modules/turnos/booking_requests_router.py`, `modules/turnos/service.mjs`, `modules/turnos/offer.mjs`, `modules/turnos/confirm.mjs`, `locales/es/admin.srv.json`, `locales/en/admin.srv.json`; tests `test/test_db_unavailable_answer.py` (`TestTheAnswers`), `test/offer-native.test.mjs`, `test/confirm-link.test.mjs`, `test/e2e/booking-unknown-outcome.spec.mjs`.

## 2026-11-10 · Public cut: neutral roles instead of people's names, a fictional DNI in the fixtures, and private-only tests left out of the cut
Decided:
- The current texts of every file that goes into the public cut name roles, not people: the product owner / responsable del producto, the reference-deployment counterpart / contraparte técnica, the on-site tester, the maintainer, the reviewer; private host names became «the dev machine» and «hsi-lab». Fixture people became fictional (Ilya Belov, `ilya@example.com`). Kept on purpose: `MAINTAINERS.md`, `GOVERNANCE.md` and `test/test_licensing_contract.py` (the public maintainer and licence facts) and `docs/legal/` (Generated Media, Inc. as owner and licensor of the code). Commit history is untouched: the public repository is one fresh commit.
- A real DNI, tied in the SISA diagnosis note to a named person and their insurer, was used as a sample in the connector samples, golden file, stories and tests: it is now the fictional 20456781 (20.456.781 dotted), and the note describes the insurers generically.
- Two roadmap evidence notes lost the reviewer's name from their file names (`docs/roadmap/audit-row13-coverage-evidence.md`, `audit-row35-queue-evidence.md`), links updated.
- Six tests that exist only to pin files of this installation which the cut does not carry (the `.claude/` hooks and settings, the root `CLAUDE.md` size, the CAPS-movil watcher) are `export-ignore` in `.gitattributes` and forbidden paths of `scripts/public_cut_policy.json`, the one list of what stays out; `test/test_public_cut_build.py` checks every test named there still exists in the working repository. Test comments that pointed at `CLAUDE.md` or the private design plans now point at `CONTRIBUTING.md`, the area's `AGENTS.md`, or describe the requirement.
- The frozen Alembic baseline `alembic/baseline/0001_schema.sql` keeps its SQL comments with people's names: it is hash-pinned and must not change; the names are in comments only.
- The seed script's sample phone is the repository's usual placeholder `+5492254000000`, not a real number.
- `docs/deploy/coolify-reference.md` stays in the cut: it is the generic Coolify walkthrough, pinned to stay generic by `test/coolify-reference-contract.test.mjs`, not this installation's operations.
Why: the preflight's salted digests found people's names and private hosts in the cut, and 42 tests of the cut named files that the cut leaves out. After this change the preflight fails only on the five honest approval records, and five tests of the cut name an excluded path, each on purpose (a 403 check on `/CLAUDE.md`, synthetic fixtures of the cut and preflight tests).
Undo: revert the commit.
Source: `.gitattributes`, `scripts/public_cut_policy.json`, `scripts/seed_patient_account.py`, `connectors/`, `docs/`, `locales/`, `test/`; tests `test/test_public_cut_build.py`, `test/test_public_tree_hygiene.py`, `test/test_public_cut_preflight.py`.

## 2026-11-11 · CI: a runner without gitleaks and an archived copy of the repository skip honestly, with the reason on the verdict line
- `scripts/check_unittest_coverage.py` (the guard both `scripts/ci_local.sh` and `.gitlab-ci.yml` run after the backend suite) used to turn every skip red except the external-pack one, so a CI runner without `gitleaks` failed on the cut's leak test. Two more exact reasons are allowed now: «gitleaks is not installed on this runner» (`test/test_public_cut_build.py` skips its gitleaks test only when the binary is absent) and «archived copy: export-ignore leaves out the private file this test needs». Each allowed reason is counted on the OK line, so a skip is visible, never silent; any other reason stays red.
- A copy built with `git archive` lacks the `export-ignore` paths (`CLAUDE.md`, the `.claude/` hooks, the targets of the six private-only tests). `test/_archive.py` is the one helper: the copy is recognised by one export-ignored hook, `.claude/hooks/managed-credential-boundary.py`, which nobody adds to a copy of their own (a user's own `CLAUDE.md` does not turn the tests back on); the six private-only tests skip as a whole module there, and the few tests of the cut that read an excluded file (the private digests, the export-ignored list, the first-admin script, the `/CLAUDE.md` 403) skip or expect 404 with the same reason. In the working repository nothing skips for it (`test/test_unittest_coverage_guard.py` pins that and the shared reason).
Why: another team's runner has no gitleaks and builds its copy with `git archive`; both made the backend run red without a failing test.
Undo: revert the commit.
Source: `scripts/check_unittest_coverage.py`, `test/_archive.py`; tests `test/test_unittest_coverage_guard.py`, `test/test_public_cut_build.py`.

## 2026-11-11 · Tests: an autovacuum worker on a test database is not a run holding it
- The stale-database sweeps (`_dbtest.reclaim_stale_databases`, `_routes_fixture.reclaim_stale_run_databases`, `scripts/lane_janitor.py`) counted every `pg_stat_activity` row under a database as a live session. An autovacuum worker analysing a freshly written test database is listed there too, so a genuinely orphaned database was spared for as long as the worker stayed. One census now, `test/_pg_sessions.py`: every backend counts except `autovacuum worker` (DROP DATABASE signals such a worker and waits about 5 s itself; any other backend makes DROP refuse, so it still counts). The janitor writes the same rule as SQL, pinned by `test/test_lane_janitor.py`.
- `test_sigkilled_run_leaves_an_orphan_a_later_run_reclaims` waits for the killed client by the same census, with its 30 s quiet-machine ceiling stretched by load (`_wait.until(scale_with_load=True)`), and retries the sweep while only autovacuum blocks the DROP; a client session that stays, or an orphan that is never dropped, still fails, now with the `pg_stat_activity` rows in the message.
Why: the test failed once in a loaded backend-full (the session did not go within 30 s) and never alone. Measured on 2026-11-11 against the colima lane (ssh port forward): the SIGKILLed client's backend leaves in 0.05-0.07 s (76 kills, quiet and under three DB-heavy test files); a non-client row (autovacuum, once seen in VacuumDelay) was still on the orphan in 3 of 76 kills.
Undo: revert the commit.
Source: `test/_pg_sessions.py`, `test/_dbtest.py`, `test/_routes_fixture.py`, `scripts/lane_janitor.py`; tests `test/test_dbtest_run_isolation.py`, `test/test_lane_janitor.py`.

## 2026-11-11 · Tests: the font-fallback spec runs Chromium without font hinting, so the font-fallback width check measures the same on Linux as on macOS
- `test/e2e/admin-font-fallback.spec.mjs` launches its Chromium (only that spec, not the whole suite) with `--font-render-hinting=none`. The red `admin-font-fallback.spec.mjs` "queue at 768 keeps its rows and width" (web font 2864 against 2735 in the fallback, 4.5 percent, deterministic in the Linux container, green on macOS) was not a wrong fallback face: `local()` resolves to Liberation Sans and Liberation Sans Bold on Linux as intended. Linux Chromium hints glyph advances to whole pixels, so the fractional `size-adjust` of the `<family> Fallback` faces (101.76, 100.19...) rounds away and the same text measures 721 px in Manrope 800 against 689 px in its fallback.
- Measured in the `mcr.microsoft.com/playwright:v1.63.0-noble` image on a 16 px sample line: with hinting Manrope 400/500/600/700/800 = 657/675/698/712/721 against fallback 656/656/689/689/689; without hinting 657/669/680/692/704 against 667/671/674/692/704 (largest gap 1.5 percent, Fira Sans 1.4 percent). The 2 percent threshold of the test is unchanged.
Why: the fallback metrics are right for the layout a visitor gets on macOS, Windows or Linux with subpixel text; the runner was the only place where hinting rounded them away, and no CSS value survives per-glyph integer rounding.
Undo: remove `launchOptions` from the spec (a hinted Linux run then fails that one test again).
Source: `test/e2e/admin-font-fallback.spec.mjs`.

### 2026-11-11 — The worker thread dump uses a non-fatal signal, not SIGABRT
Decided: `test/_workerproc.WorkerProc` no longer sends SIGABRT to an overrunning worker. Every test worker child gets `test/_fh_site/sitecustomize.py` through PYTHONPATH (test-only, never production), which calls `faulthandler.register(SIGUSR2, all_threads=True)` and then runs any sitecustomize it shadows; `stop()` sends SIGUSR2, waits for the stacks on stderr, then the unchanged SIGKILL. It replaces `PYTHONFAULTHANDLER=1` + SIGABRT of the 2026-11-07 entry; the failure message still carries the stack of every thread (`test/test_workerproc_thread_dump.py`).
Why: every SIGABRT of a python process makes macOS write a crash report and show «Python quit unexpectedly»; seven windows appeared on the owner screen after midnight, all from `keeper_holds_the_lock` of that test. `faulthandler.register` is still a C handler (writes even when the main thread is stuck in C code), unlike the Python signal handler rejected earlier.
Undo: revert the commit.
Source: `test/_workerproc.py`, `test/_fh_site/sitecustomize.py`, `test/test_workerproc_thread_dump.py`.

### 2026-11-11 — The extension's question under «Cargar en HSI» survives a repaint of the list
Decided: when the press asks a question (sex or name missing), `preguntar()` in `extensions/hsi-humanizador/content-turnos.js` no longer gives up if the row's control is not on the page at that instant; it draws the question at the top of the page. Before, a repaint of the queue that fell between the press and the question took the control away for a beat, the press ended as «reposo» and nothing was loaded, so the click was lost (the flaky `queue row (telefono)` of `hsi-sync-entries.spec.mjs`: 7 of 17 red at `--workers=4 --repeat-each=12`, 0 of 96 after).
Why: a staff click that silently does nothing is worse than a question in a slightly different place.
Undo: revert the commit.
Source: `extensions/hsi-humanizador/content-turnos.js`, `test/e2e/hsi-sync-entries.spec.mjs`.

### 2026-11-11 — HSI Humanizador 0.7.4 published to the Chrome Web Store
Decided: the question-under-«Cargar en HSI» fix goes out as extension 0.7.4 (manifest bumped, `extensions/dist` rebuilt, package uploaded and submitted through `scripts/publish_extension.py --publish`); the store draft reports `crxVersion` 0.7.4.
Why: the fix lived only in main, so the hospital's staff, who get the extension from the store, did not have it.
Undo: publish a later version; the store keeps no rollback.
Source: `extensions/hsi-humanizador/manifest.json`, `extensions/hsi-humanizador/README.md`.

### 2026-11-11 — J18 rejected-request spec books the third day of the grid, not the first
Decided: the first J18 test takes the earliest time of the grid's third day instead of the first day; the spec asserts the grid has at least three days. The other journeys already skip the first day.
Why: the earliest time of today can become past while the run crosses the slot boundary, so the offer after the rejection started later than the time asked for (full CI, 11:00 ART).
Undo: revert to the first day, accepting the flake.
Source: `test/e2e/journey-j18-reject-decline-restore.spec.mjs`.

### 2026-11-11 — A late web font no longer wraps the queue's patient line for a frame
Decided: while the page's web font is pending, `html.tbl-fuentes` (set by `static/platform/admin-table.mjs`, cleared in the microtask after the refit that follows `loadingdone`/`loadingerror`; a failed face counts as settled) keeps `.queue-pat-line` and its name link on one line (`static/platform/admin-queue.css`). The font-fallback e2e is split into one spec per theme (`admin-font-fallback-<theme>.spec.mjs`, tests in `test/e2e/lib/font-fallback-suite.mjs`), each on its own database, because the theme lives in the database and parallel workers running the two themes flipped it under each other; each test waits for its own theme to paint and for that theme's font.
Why: the shift log of the red runs (CLS 0.026-0.034 at 1280) showed the patient line wrapping on the last pixel in the new face and the rows growing 62 to 84 px and back 15 ms later, when the refit arrived; the 0.02 threshold stays. Accepted: a very long name overflows its cell for those few frames instead of wrapping. Other tables do not use the class yet.

Decided: the `html.tbl-fuentes` nowrap rules in `static/platform/admin-queue.css` apply only from 1025px up (the queue's tablet layout ends at 1024px), and `marcarFuentes` in `static/platform/admin-table.mjs` drops the class 3 s after it was first set even if the font never reports (`document.fonts.check` stays false when a face is blocked or never requested).

Decided: inside `booking_horizon.staff()` (the desk grid, `/api/admin/appointment-slots`) `site_availability._agenda_day_hours` no longer drops windows with `cupos.web: 0`; the patient site and the wizard, outside `staff()`, still do. No visual distinction on the grid, as in «Pacientes de hoy».
Why: the desk and the doctor keep those hours for phone bookings and their own patients; «Pacientes de hoy» (`doctor_appointments._minutes_of_day`) already shows them free, but «Crear turno» could not book them. One day-hours function with a `staff()` switch, not a second calculation beside it.
