### 2026-11-11 — An appointment created straight in HSI takes its hour here too
Decided: a mirrored HSI appointment (not cancelled, in the future, linked to no local request) makes the doctor's minute busy wherever free hours are counted: the public site and wizard (`slot_holds`), approval and «Crear turno» (`booking_capacity`, overbooks count it as an ordinary busy turn) and «Pacientes de hoy» (`_free_of_day`). One reader, `modules/turnos/hsi_busy.py`: the diary's professional must resolve to exactly one catalog doctor (`resolve_doctor_id`) and the minute must fall inside that doctor's agenda of a booking service; otherwise it takes nothing and never raises. With `TURNOS_HSI_SYNC_OFF=1` or no EMR routed it is empty.
Why: the desk books in HSI while the site kept offering the same hour, so two patients could arrive for one hour.
Accepted: an HSI turn of a doctor whose HSI name matches two catalog doctors (or none) still takes nothing; matching by name is the only link between the two systems.
Undo: revert the commit; no schema change.
Source: `modules/turnos/hsi_busy.py`, `core/db_hsi.py`, `modules/turnos/slot_holds.py`, `modules/turnos/booking_capacity.py`, `modules/turnos/doctor_appointments.py`, `admin_panel/admin_appointments.py`, `docs/architecture/hsi.md`.
## 2026-11-11 · Dependencies: pydantic is pinned, and the private SQLAlchemy API of the query bridge is checked by a test
- `requirements.txt` pins `pydantic==2.14.3`: the application imports it directly (`core/api_body.py`, every `*/api_models.py`), and until now it came in through FastAPI's range, so an image built on another day could run another release. 2.14.3 is the latest release (2026-11-05), what a fresh install of that range resolves to; pydantic pins its own `pydantic-core` exactly.
- `core/sqlcore.py` compiles a Core statement outside an `Engine` with three private SQLAlchemy members (`ClauseElement._compile_w_cache`, `SQLCompiler._process_parameters_for_postcompile`, `SQLCompiler._bind_processors`) and one private keyword (`construct_params(_no_postcompile=True)`). `sqlcore.SQLALCHEMY_VERIFIED` names the release they were checked on; `test/test_dependency_pins.py` fails when the `requirements.txt` pin differs from it, when `sqlcore.py` touches a private name not in its inventory, or when a listed member is missing or changed shape on the installed release. The installed version itself is not compared with the pin: a developer's machine may carry another release, and the shape checks are what matter there.
- README, «Tech stack»: why the queries are SQLAlchemy Core on the psycopg cursor, without an `Engine` or a `Session`.
Why: TASK-76 (bringing the code to the FastAPI/Pydantic/SQLAlchemy standards before the repository is opened, owner's decision 2026-11-11), item A. Nothing a patient or the desk sees changes.
Rejected: comparing the installed SQLAlchemy/pydantic with the pin in the test (red on any machine with another release, while the API the bridge calls may be identical); moving the bridge onto a public `Engine`/`Connection` (a second pool beside `psycopg_pool`, a rewrite of the DB layer's timeouts and outage answers for no behaviour gained).
Undo: revert the commit.
Source: `requirements.txt`, `core/sqlcore.py`, `README.md`; test `test/test_dependency_pins.py`.
By: hospital agent

## 2026-11-11 · The signed-in patient is a FastAPI dependency (`cuentas/account_deps.py`)
- `current_account` (the account, or the 401 `{"error": "no_autenticado"}` the account routes built by hand, byte for byte: `core.deps.PatientNotSignedIn`, an `HTTPException`, rendered by the handler `deps.install_gate_handlers` registers with the staff gates' own, so every app or test app that has those has this one too) and `optional_account` (the account or None). Both are `accounts.current_user(request)` off the event loop, read once per request.
- In the signature now: `GET`/`POST /api/account/dependents`, `POST /api/account/update`, `POST /api/account/dependents/delete` (`current_account`; `_require_session` is gone, `accounts._dependents_core` takes the account); `GET /api/chat/history`, `POST /api/chat/new`, `/api/account/mcp` and `/api/account/mcp/regenerate` (both spellings), `GET /api/account/lab-results` (`optional_account`: they answer the anonymous visitor themselves — the lab family its own `{"error": "auth_requerida"}` — or serve it). 11 route-methods; hand-written session reads in router files: 12 -> 7.
- Kept by hand, because a dependency runs first and would read the session where the route does not yet: `GET /account` and its HTML family (the `?lang=` switch answers first), a chat turn (`/api/chat/stream` answers 413 to an oversized body first), `/service/<id>/book` and the account redirects of the patient site (the language switch and the module gate first), the lab link (404 with no provider key first), the lab upload and delete (FastAPI's 422 for a missing file or a non-numeric id first), and `/api/account/me` (503 on a database outage, where the dependency answers 401). Where the patient portal login is on, reading the session can create or bind the account (`accounts.current_user`), so it must not move ahead of an answer that came before it. `test/test_account_deps.py` holds this inventory: a new hand-written read in a router is red.
- A dependency only for a route without typed parameters or a declared body: FastAPI validates those after the dependencies, so the session would be read before their 422, and a raised 401 would replace it.
Why: TASK-76, item H. No answer changes: the same status, body and headers in every case, and the session is read at the same point of each request as before.
Rejected: a 401 from FastAPI's own `HTTPException` (its body is `{"detail": ...}`, not the routes'); a custom `APIRoute` class catching the exception per router (less usual than an exception handler, which the staff gates already use).
Undo: revert the commit.
Source: `cuentas/account_deps.py`, `cuentas/accounts_router.py`, `cuentas/accounts.py`, `modules/chat/chat_api_router.py`, `modules/chat/mcp_api_router.py`, `ai/lab_results_router.py`, `core/deps.py`, `docs/architecture/cuentas-y-cobertura.md`; test `test/test_account_deps.py`.
By: hospital agent

## 2026-11-11 · OpenAPI: groups by owning package, info, summaries, the two session cookies and the common answers
- `routers/api_docs.build_schema` (served at `/openapi.json` to an admin only, as before) now puts every operation in exactly one group — the package or app module its endpoint is defined in (`TAGS`: Patient site, Appointments, Patient accounts, Pre-visit interview, Chat, AI, Staff sign-in, Staff panel, Installation, Connectors, Platform, Legacy URLs); a package with routes and no group is an error, not an untagged operation. The group comes from the owning package in one place rather than a `tags=` repeated on 99 routers: one source for one datum.
- `info`: a description (what the API is, the two cookies, the flat `{"error": ...}` answer and its status codes), the AGPL-3.0 license, and the build's commit (`core/version.short()`) as the version instead of FastAPI's default `0.1.0`. Every operation gets a summary from its route's name (`_me_endpoint` -> «Me»); a name of generic words only (a factory's `_html_endpoint`) gives the path.
- Security schemes `staffSession` (`st_admin`) and `patientSession` (`st_ses`) are `APIKeyCookie(auto_error=False)` objects declared by the gates themselves (`core.deps.STAFF_SESSION` in `require_admin`/`require_admin_screen`, `cuentas.account_deps.PATIENT_SESSION`), so FastAPI lists them on every operation behind a declared gate; they read the cookie and decide nothing. The answers of the gate are documented with them: 401/403 `ApiError` for a staff API, 303/403 for a panel screen, 401 for `account_deps.current_account`; every `/api/` operation (243) documents the 503 of a database that does not answer.
- A handler that gates by hand — it awaits `deps.check_admin` or its router's own gate helper, usually to keep a check ahead of the gate, or reads the patient's session itself and answers 401 — carries a documentation marker in its registration: `openapi_extra=deps.STAFF_GATE_DOCS` (72 operations) or `deps.PATIENT_SESSION_DOCS` (17), `| ...` after the `api_body.docs(...)` it already had; a route an anonymous visitor may use too has `deps.PATIENT_SESSION_OPTIONAL_DOCS` (security `[{}, {"patientSession": []}]`). The markers are documentation only (an `x-turnos-*` key `routers/api_docs.py` turns into the security and the answers). Operations documenting a session: 0 -> 207 of 428.
- Held by an anonymous probe in `test/test_api_docs_schema.py`: every `/api/`, `/admin` and `/mcp` operation (but the chat stream) gets an anonymous request with no database and no network; one that answers 401 must document a session, and one that documents a required session must not answer 2xx. Left out on purpose: `GET /entrevista/{sid}`, a page that takes the patient's session or the link's own token.
- `scripts/export_openapi.py [--out FILE]` writes the same schema without a server or a database.
Why: TASK-76, item B: 374 paths in one untagged list, no security, summaries like « Html Endpoint».
Rejected: `tags=[...]` on each of the 99 `APIRouter`s (the same fact in 99 places, kept equal only by a test); listing the staff session on every `/api/admin` path by its prefix (a claim the code does not make — the probe shows which answer 401); a no-op `Depends(STAFF_SESSION)` on the hand-gated routes (it would read like enforcement that is not there).
Undo: revert the commit.
Source: `routers/api_docs.py`, `core/deps.py`, `cuentas/account_deps.py`, the routers that gate by hand, `scripts/export_openapi.py`, `README.md`; test `test/test_api_docs_schema.py`.
By: hospital agent

## 2026-11-11 · A new staff cookie is never zlib-compressed
- `auth/admin_auth._itsdangerous_serializer()` signs with `_PlainPayloadSerializer`, an `itsdangerous.URLSafeTimedSerializer` whose `dump_payload` skips the compression: the cookie's first segment is always base64 JSON. itsdangerous compressed whenever zlib came out shorter, which with this payload depends on the account and on the random `nonce` (`secrets.token_urlsafe(24)`), measured on 5000 payloads each: a username equal to its role (the owner's `admin`/`admin`, `reportes`/`reportes`) and every renewed cookie (`sid` beside the `nonce`) compressed always; a fresh cookie of the e2e desk account (`e2e-desk`/`admisionista`) about 1 in 170, of a long doctor username about 1 in 10. A compressed cookie starts with `.` and compressed bytes.
- So most cookies change shape — now all are plain — and nothing else: same name, attributes, signature, salt and claims; a plain cookie is 1 to 21 characters longer than the compressed one it replaces. itsdangerous reads both forms, so a compressed cookie handed out before still verifies, and a plain one still verifies on a release from before this change (rollback). The session id, its revocation and the renewal read the claims, not the form. Nothing else in the code looks at the form (searched for a leading `.` and for zlib): only the tests below and e2e J28.1.
Why: `test_session_revocation.TestSessionLineage.test_a_fresh_cookie_keeps_its_old_shape_and_its_nonce_is_the_session_id` failed at random (caught in a full CI run, green on a rerun), and the e2e journey J28.1 reads the same segment as base64 JSON to forge a promoted copy, so it shared the same rare failure. The test pins what the journeys rely on; the code now guarantees it instead of a lucky draw.
Rejected: retrying the login in the test until the cookie comes out uncompressed (hides the same failure in J28); dropping the check (the e2e journeys still read the segment).
Undo: revert the commit (cookies issued in between stay valid either way).
Source: `auth/admin_auth.py`; tests `test/test_session_revocation.py` (a nonce that repeats the username makes zlib win on every run; the owner's fresh cookie and a renewed one are plain; a compressed cookie made by the stock serializer still signs in).
By: hospital agent

## 2026-11-11 · OpenAPI: the last request bodies, and the query parameters every API operation reads
- Body models for the write operations that still had none in the schema: the panel's browser error reports (`ClientErrorIn`), the table export (`TableExportIn`), the «Recorrido» steps (`FunnelStepsIn`), the AI register notice (`AiRegisterNotifyIn`), the usability findings and their analysis (`UsabilityFindingIn`, `UsabilityFindingUpdateIn`, `UsabilityAnalyzeIn`), a doctor's or a centre's photo and the doctor's own (`PhotoUploadIn`), the chart summary regenerated (`ai/api_models.PatientSummaryIn`), a chat turn, streamed or not (`modules/chat/api_models.ChatMessageIn`), the voice dictation (`VoiceIn`) and the usability study (`StudySessionIn`, `StudyEventsIn`, `StudyResultIn`, `StudyFinishIn`). The doctors' list import takes a file, not JSON: `api_body.RawBody` documents it by its media types. Write operations with a request body in the schema: 72 -> 91 of 148.
- The 57 that have none are listed in `test/test_api_docs_schema.NO_REQUEST_BODY` with the reason: an action whose path and session say everything (47, among them every `DELETE` that shares its route with a `PUT`/`POST`), the catch-all of paths no route serves (4), and three that read a body this API does not describe — the MCP connection (JSON-RPC), WhatsApp's webhook (the provider's payload) and the first-run installer (HTML forms).
- The query parameters each route reads are documented per method with `api_body.query_docs(get={"days": api_body.Query(...)})`, turned into the operation's `parameters` by `routers/api_docs.apply_query_docs`: 45 `/api` operations, 0 -> 75 parameters, each optional, with what it does and what an unusable value falls back to. Documentation only: the routes keep reading them from the request with their own lenient parsing; a FastAPI `Query()` in the signature would answer 422 to a value they now treat as absent. `?lang=` (the language switch that answers first on the patient site's JSON and the door poster) is one shared description, `api_body.LANG_SWITCH`. Pages are left out: they are not the API.
- Both are checked against the handlers' source, not a list kept by hand: `test/_request_reads.py` walks a handler for one method (its branches on `request.method`, the helpers, closures and domain functions it hands the request or the query to, function-level imports) and says whether it reads the body and which query names. The guard in `test/test_api_docs_schema.py` fails when a write operation reads a body and documents none, when a listed action reads one, when a documented JSON body is not read, and when an `/api` operation documents other query parameters than the ones its handler reads.
- Pinned by `test/routes/test_body_contract_tools.py` (`test/golden/body_contract_tools.json`, recorded on the routes before this change): 16 routes, 399 answers to the body battery, unchanged byte for byte.
Why: TASK-76 (the code to the FastAPI/Pydantic/SQLAlchemy standards before the repository is opened, owner's decision 2026-11-11), item C. Nothing a patient or the desk sees changes.
Rejected: `Query()` parameters in the signatures (they change the answer to a bad value: item G decides that); a `requestBody` for the MCP and WhatsApp protocols (their payloads are defined elsewhere); counting body reads and query names with a text search (it misses `run_in_threadpool(fn, request)`, closures and a query handed to a domain function).
Undo: revert the commit.
Source: `core/api_body.py`, `routers/api_docs.py`, the routers and `*/api_models.py` named above, `test/admin-doctor-patients.test.mjs`; tests `test/test_api_docs_schema.py`, `test/test_api_body.py`, `test/routes/test_body_contract_tools.py`; helper `test/_request_reads.py`.
By: hospital agent

## 2026-11-11 · The last bodies go through their models; the chart summary answers 400, not 500, to a body that is not an object
- `POST /api/admin/patient-summary` decoded its body itself and called `.get` on it: JSON that is not an object (`[]`, `null`, `"x"`, `123`) was a bare 500 «Internal Server Error». The body now goes through `ai/api_models.PatientSummaryIn` (`api_body.checked` + `api_body.call`, contract `ai/api_models.BODY`): those four answer 400 `{"error": "JSON inválido: el cuerpo: se esperaba un objeto"}` (`Cache-Control: no-store`). A malformed body still reads as `{}` and answers «falta dni», as before. This is the only answer that changes: the 4 cases are the only diff of `test/golden/body_contract_tools.json`, which now holds no 500 (`test_no_body_crashes_a_route`).
- The other new models check the body their routes already decoded, without touching it or the answer: `api_body.watch(request, value, model)` logs `body_contract_drift` (route, field path, error type, never a value) — the browser error reports, the table export, the «Recorrido» steps, the AI register notice, the usability analysis and findings, the study's four calls, both photo uploads, a chat turn (streamed or not) and the dictation. None of them crashes on any body of the battery (the golden), so none needs `run`/`call`; the drift is what a later strict boundary (TASK-76 item F) is decided on.
Why: TASK-76, item C, and hospital's rule since the Pydantic stages: no 500 on garbage input.
Rejected: `api_body.run`/`call` around every domain call (the study family answers its own 422 to a `ValueError`/`TypeError`: wrapping would turn some of those into 400); `observe()` on the raw bytes of the chat and the photos (a second JSON decode of a multi-megabyte body; `watch()` checks the value already decoded).
Undo: revert the commit (the 4 cases are a 500 again).
Source: `core/api_body.py`, `ai/api_models.py`, `ai/chart_summary_router.py` and the routers named above; tests `test/test_api_body.py` (`TestWatch`), `test/routes/test_body_contract_tools.py`.
By: hospital agent

## 2026-11-11 · Routes take their body and their query parameters through their signature
- The request body is a dependency of the route's signature, `body: api_body.Body = Depends(api_body.body(CONTRACT, BodyIn))`, instead of a reader called in the handler (`read_model`, `lazy_model`, `observe`, `checked`, `watch`): 79 routes. FastAPI resolves dependencies before the handler in the order of the signature, after the route's own `dependencies=`, so whatever answered before the body is a dependency declared ahead of it — the gate (`core.native_gate.staff`, the same `gate()` as a dependency), a path that names no item, a closed entry, a per-IP limit. A dependency with an answer of its own raises `core.deps.EarlyResponse(response)`, and the app's handler sends that response as it is; the bytes are the ones the handler returned before.
- The factories in `core/api_body.py`: `body()` (read and checked before the handler: 44 routes), `raw()` (the bytes for a domain that decodes them itself, `observe()` on a copy: 9), `lazy()` (a `LazyBody` the domain decodes after its own checks: 5), `lenient()` (a family that reads a body that does not decode as `{}`: 6), `watched()` (a family's own bounded reader, the study's: 6) and `deferred()` (9). A `deferred()` body is read by the dependency — reading answers nothing — and decoded by the handler with `.read()` at the point it always was, because the checks ahead of it need the handler: the request row it loaded, a rate limit it spent, a gate whose flags depend on the method. `test/test_body_query_deps.DECODED_IN_THE_HANDLER` lists those nine with what comes first.
- The query parameters a route reads are typed parameters of its signature, `dia: str | None = Query(None, description=...)`: always text, so FastAPI never refuses one (no 422), and parsed as leniently as before. FastAPI takes the last value of a repeated parameter, exactly as `request.query_params.get` did. `api_body.query_docs` still documents a parameter the signature cannot describe as it is — one of a route object that serves several methods (FastAPI would list it on every method) or a number read leniently from text — and the signature declares it `include_in_schema=False`. `routers/api_docs.py` documents an optional text parameter as `string` (not FastAPI's `anyOf [string, null]`) and drops FastAPI's 422 from an operation whose only parameters are such.
- `test/test_body_query_deps.py` walks the shipped Python (aliases, function-level imports, closures, `request.method` branches, a reader handed along uncalled) and lists every function that still reads the body or the query by hand: 151 on the base, 54 now, each in `STAYS_BY_HAND` with why — the domain takes the `parse_qs` mapping (the FIRST value of a repeated parameter: a typed parameter would take the last; the reports, the queue, the patients list, the slots), the language switch, the post-sign-in `next` boundary, a size cap answered before the body is read, a bounded streaming reader, a protocol of its own (chat, MCP, WhatsApp, the installer's form), a typed path parameter that FastAPI validates after the dependencies.
- What changes for a client: nothing that answers. The bodies of the pinned batteries (`test/golden/body_contract_*.json`, 399 answers on the 16 routes of item C among them) are byte for byte the same; the OpenAPI schema keeps every body, parameter, answer and security scheme it had and documents 6 query parameters it did not (`/account/portal-login?next`, `/admin/reset-password?token`, `/admin/cola/print?dia,medico,auto`, `/study?role`). Two things move where nobody reads the answer: a client that hangs up while sending its body now meets the app's 499 also where a route used to swallow the disconnect as an empty body (settings, branding) or to answer a 404 before reading it; the bytes of a route whose own checks came first are now read before them.
Why: TASK-76 (the code to the FastAPI/Pydantic/SQLAlchemy standards before the repository is opened, the owner's decision 2026-11-11), items E and G.
Rejected: `body: Model` in the signature (FastAPI decodes and validates before the gate, and decodes differently); typed `int` or `Literal` query parameters (a bad value would be a 422); typed parameters for the `parse_qs` routes (a repeated parameter would take another value); moving every check ahead of the body into a dependency (the nine `deferred()` routes need what their handler loaded).
Undo: revert the commit.
Source: `core/api_body.py`, `core/deps.py` (`EarlyResponse`), `core/native_gate.py` (`staff`), `routers/api_docs.py` and the routers named in the test's inventories; tests `test/test_body_query_deps.py`, `test/test_api_docs_schema.py` (with `test/_request_reads.py`, which now reads the signature too), `test/routes/test_body_contract_*.py`.
By: hospital agent

## 2026-11-11 · Process keys: typed pydantic-settings classes, read lazily, nothing else reads them
- The 26 keys that decide how a process runs before (or without) the database are fields of five `BaseSettings` classes in `core/settings.py`, one per read policy: `ServerSettings` (`HOST`, `PORT`), `ProcessSettings` (ASGI switch, queue worker topology, thread limiter, pool size and wait, web statement/lock limits, worker drain and health port, outbound kill switch, `TURNOS_CONFIG_LOCK`, `TURNOS_ENV_FILE`, timezone, legacy webdata dir, Sentry DSN/env/signals), `WorkerSettings` (`TURNOS_WORKER_STALLED_S`, alone: a non-number is exit 2 and must not break the other reads), `DatabaseSettings` (`DATABASE_URL`, `TURNOS_DB_MIGRATION_URL`: environment, then the env file, whitespace-only = unset), `InstallationSettings` (`TURNOS_PUBLIC_ORIGIN` and the three install-owned pack keys: environment, then the env file, empty = unset). The 28 direct `os.environ` reads of those keys in `core/db.py`, `core/db_schema.py`, `core/concurrency.py`, `core/config.py`, `core/queue_infra.py`, `core/outbound_guard.py`, `core/proxy_trust.py`, `core/tz.py`, `core/sitedata.py`, `core/data_packs.py`, `core/error_tracking.py`, `core/launch_signals.py`, `core/admin_csrf_middleware.py`, `asgi_main.py` and `worker.py` now read the class; two more stay on the environment on purpose (the exit-2 log line that echoes an unreadable `TURNOS_WORKER_STALLED_S`, and `outbound_guard`'s fallback for when `core.db` itself cannot be imported).
- Lazy: `XSettings.current()` reads the raw values on every call and rebuilds the frozen instance only when one changed (the memo is keyed by the raw values, env file included; an instance whose values moved while it was built is not memoized), so the ~1250 environment edits of the suite need no reset and the panel-free hot readers (`config.locked()`, `data_packs.resolve_path`) cost a few dictionary lookups.
- Every validator is the parse its call site had (blank/garbage → default, sizes never below 1, the pool wait capped at 60 s, a limit of 0 = none, `== "1"` exact or after a strip); `test/test_process_settings.py` holds each migrated reader against a copy of the reader as it was on main 571f7bd3 over a battery of raw values (and, for the two file-backed classes, every pair environment × file), and fails when product code reads a declared key from the environment itself.
- `config.get()` with its database layer, 3-second cache and lock is untouched. The `TURNOS_*` registry also lists the settings fields, with the class as the reader and the field default (`HOST`, `PORT` and `DATABASE_URL` gain rows). Gone with their last caller: `concurrency.env_size` (its parse is `settings.size_or`) and `data_packs._boot_setting` (`_boot_settings()` returns the class); `db._timeout_ms` takes the parsed seconds.
- Pins: `pydantic-settings==2.16.2` and its `python-dotenv==1.2.4` (already pulled by `uvicorn[standard]`, unpinned until now).
- No behaviour changes: a `PORT` that is not an integer still stops the web boot with `int()`'s own `ValueError`, an unreadable `TURNOS_WORKER_STALLED_S` raises `float()`'s own error and is still exit 2 with the same log line (`current()` raises the parse's original error, not pydantic's wrapper). One residue, by design of a class read as a whole: the env file is opened when ANY field of a file-backed class is unset in the environment (before, each reader opened it only for its own key) — a read of `config.dotenv_values()`, which every `config.get()` that falls through does anyway.
Rejected: one class for all keys (a class validates as a whole, so a bad `PORT` would break every other read, the worker's included); caching `get_settings()` with `lru_cache` (tests and operators change the environment at run time); pydantic-settings' own dotenv source (its parser differs from `config`'s); a database source for `config.get` (its cache and lock are proven, a rewrite gains nothing).
Why: TASK-76 item I — «textbook» FastAPI/Pydantic: process configuration typed and declared in one place.
Undo: revert the commit (no migration, no data).
By: hospital agent
Source: `core/settings.py`, `scripts/env_registry.py`, `docs/env-registry.md`, `docs/architecture/settings.md`; tests `test/test_process_settings.py`, `test/test_env_registry.py`.

Decided: `POST /api/admin/content/{tipo}/{id}/restaurar` for an id with no base item and no overlay row (never existed, or a custom item already hard-deleted) answers 404 `{"error": "no_encontrado"}` — the shape the same family already gives for an item the editor may not touch (`edit_item`). It was 200 `{"ok": true, "item": null}`. Restoring a base item, un-deleting a hidden row and restoring a live custom item answer as before.
Why: the product owner's rule for staff screens — what is not there (or not yours) is a 404; a 200 told the panel a restore had happened.
Rejected: the terminal route 404 `{"error": "not found"}` (that one is for a path the router declines before any auth check; this one is a domain answer after the admin gate).
Undo: revert the commit.
By: hospital agent
Source: `admin_panel/admin_content.py` (`_restore_item`), `admin_panel/api_models.py` (`ContentItemOut`: its docstring no longer promises a null `item`); tests `test/test_admin_content.py` (`test_restaurar_id_inexistente_es_404`, `test_restaurar_item_propio_borrado_en_duro_es_404`, `test_restaurar_item_propio_vivo_sigue_200`).

## 2026-11-11 · «Invitar médico» without an email on a portal-login installation is a 400 before anything is written
Decided: on an installation with `TURNOS_STAFF_PORTAL_LOGIN=1`, `POST /api/admin/content/doctors` with `_invitar: true` and no email answers 400 `{"error": <admin.srv.users.portal_email_requerido>, "field": "email"}` (the existing «Escribí el email con el que entra al portal.»), checked next to the existing 409 for an email already in use, before the doctor and its account are created (`admin_users.portal_email_missing`). Before, the doctor and its inactive account were committed and then `invite_account` raised `ValueError` for the same reason outside the transaction: a 500 with a half-made doctor left behind. A plain create without an email, and every installation without the portal, answer as before.
Why: the code already says an invitation without an email cannot exist there — `invite_account` refuses it («the email IS the portal match; there is no link to hand over instead»), `create_user` answers the same 400 at the field before writing, and the list import refuses such a row before creating it (`doctor_import._plan`). The panel's «Invitar médico» form paints the answer's `error` as it does for the 409. No new text.
Rejected: creating the doctor and answering success with «invitation not sent» (a portal doctor without an email can never be invited or log in; the import and `create_user` already refuse it, and a second shape for the same refusal would be a fork); catching the `ValueError` after the commit (still a doctor nobody asked for).
Undo: revert the commit.
By: hospital agent
Source: `admin_panel/admin_content.py` (`create_item`), `admin_panel/admin_users.py` (`portal_email_missing`), `docs/architecture/admin-panel-y-auth.md`; tests `test/test_doctor_account_unify.py` (`PortalInstallationInviteTest.test_an_invitation_without_email_is_refused_before_anything_is_written`).

## 2026-11-11 · The staff's request row no longer carries `confirm_token`
Decided: `booking_requests._admin_row` drops `confirm_token`, so the queue (`GET /api/admin/requests`) and every action that answers the row (`approve`, `assign`, `reject`, `cancel`, `restore`, `attendance`, `retry-hsi`) no longer send it. The token is the patient's authentication on the reminder's «¿vas a poder venir?» page (`/appointment/confirm/<token>`) and the one-click calendar download (`/appointment/<token>.ics`), a secret in `personal_data.REGISTRY`. Nothing on the staff side reads it: not the panel (`admin-app.js`, `static/platform/admin-queue.mjs`, `admin-patients.mjs`, `admin-content.mjs`), not the HSI extension, not a template; the emails and WhatsApp mint and read it from the database (`db_booking.booking_request_confirm_token`, `booking_requests_to_remind`), never from this row. Every other field of the row is unchanged, and the documented row (`api_models.StaffRequest`, OpenAPI) no longer names the key.
Why: a staff answer handed every desk session a key that lets anyone confirm or cancel the patient's appointment and read its date, with no reader on the staff side.
Rejected: keeping it for a possible «copy the confirm link» action (none exists; the desk confirms through its own attendance actions).
Undo: revert the commit.
By: hospital agent
Source: `modules/turnos/booking_requests.py` (`_admin_row`), `test/golden/body_contract_admin_requests.json` (the key removed from the 20 bodies of `approve`, `assign` and `reject` that carry the row, nothing else changed); tests `test/test_booking_requests.py` (`TestTieneOrden.test_nunca_expone_la_llave_del_link_de_confirmacion`), `test/routes/test_body_contract_admin_requests.py`.

## 2026-11-11 · Tests: the four OpenAPI answer areas share one set of answer checks
Decided: the helpers that read a documented success answer and check an answer against its model live once, in `test/_response_models.py`: `success_answer`, `undeclared_keys`, `check_answer` (now also for a `RootModel`: a list of objects is checked row by row, a map keyed by data as a whole), `is_answer_model`, and the golden answers by route template (`golden_by_template`, `pinned_by_template`). `test/_staff_response_models.py` (area b) and `test/_settings_area.py` (area d) keep only their boundaries and exclusions; their tests call the shared helpers. Area a keeps its own golden reading by literal path, which its routes need.
Why: the four areas were written on parallel branches; b carried copies of four helpers of a, and d a copy of b's golden reading and of a's key check with the `RootModel` case added. Assembled, one copy each is what a reader expects and what a later fix has to touch once.
Undo: revert the commit (tests only).
By: hospital agent
Source: `test/_response_models.py`, `test/_staff_response_models.py`, `test/_settings_area.py`, `test/test_staff_response_docs.py`, `test/test_settings_response_docs.py`, `test/routes/test_staff_response_models.py`, `test/routes/test_settings_response_models.py`.

## 2026-11-11 · Tests: the whole-schema answer guard requires all four area lists
Decided: `test/test_openapi_response_coverage.py` imports the lists of the four areas (a `_response_models`, b `_staff_response_models`, c `_insight_area`, d `_settings_area`) directly. The fallback for an area whose file was not in the tree yet (its operations checked by d's restatement alone, their answers not asked) is gone: every operation is held by exactly one area's own list, and every one documents its success answer or is an exclusion with its reason.
Why: the fallback existed only because the four areas were built on parallel branches; assembled, a missing list would silently shrink the check instead of failing.
Undo: revert the commit (tests only).
By: hospital agent
Source: `test/test_openapi_response_coverage.py`, `test/_settings_area.py` (docstring).

## 2026-11-11 · The staff's request row no longer carries `idem_key` either
Decided: `booking_requests._admin_row` drops `idem_key` too, from the queue (`GET /api/admin/requests`) and every action that answers the row (`approve`, `assign`, `reject`, `cancel`, `restore`, `attendance`, `retry-hsi`). The key is the patient wizard's hold token, sent once in the body of `POST /api/requests` (`modules/turnos/service.mjs`) so a doubled «Confirmá» tap gets the same request back; a secret in `personal_data.REGISTRY`. Its only readers are on the database: `booking_create._idem_answer` (`db_slots.get_booking_request_by_idem_key`) and the request transfer (`scripts/fixes/requests_transfer.py`). The panel's actions have no idempotency key of their own (`admin_appointments` says so), and neither the panel scripts, the HSI extension, the MCP API, a template nor any server code over `_admin_row` reads it. Every other field of the row is unchanged.
Why: same reason as `confirm_token` (the entry «The staff's request row no longer carries `confirm_token`») — a patient secret in a staff answer with no reader there.
Undo: revert the commit.
By: hospital agent
Source: `modules/turnos/booking_requests.py` (`_admin_row`), `modules/turnos/api_models.py` (the documented row, `StaffRequest`, no longer names the key), `test/golden/body_contract_admin_requests.json` (the key removed from the same 20 bodies of `approve`, `assign` and `reject`; the patient golden `body_contract_booking.json` keeps its two body-validation cases for `idem_key`); tests `test/test_booking_requests.py` (`TestTieneOrden.test_nunca_expone_la_llave_de_idempotencia_del_paciente`), `test/routes/test_body_contract_admin_requests.py`.

## 2026-11-11 · A refusal ahead of the handler's own read is answered before the body arrives again
Decided: `api_body.deferred()` and `api_body.lazy()` read nothing in the dependency. They hand the handler a `PendingBody` bound to the request, and the handler reads the bytes where it read them before the body moved into the signature: `await pending.read()` (decode and model check), `await pending.load()` (the bytes, for `coverage`'s 16 KB cap ahead of the decode), `await pending.lazy()` (the domain's `LazyBody`). On the 12 routes whose own checks answer before the body — `POST /api/requests` (401), `/api/requests/{id}/coverage`, `/reprogramar`, `/waitlist` (404 id), `/api/intake/qr/{code}/start`, `/api/intake/t/{token}/message`, `/api/intake/case/{token}/message` (404 shape or token, 429 limit, 409 closed), `POST`/`PUT /api/admin/content/...` (404 type or action, 401/403), `POST /api/admin/patients` (403 for a doctor), `POST /api/admin/requests/{id}/attendance` (404, 401/403) — the refusal goes out without reading a byte of the body, as on `main` before this campaign; the knowledge `golden` action, which takes no body, reads none. `analyze-order` and `assign` read where they always did.
Why: with the read in the dependency (the entry «Routes take their body and their query parameters through their signature»), those refusals waited for the whole body: a 100 kB body sent over 2 s was refused after 2.0 s instead of in milliseconds, a 32 MiB one was buffered (about +62 MiB of peak memory in a local probe) before a 401/404/429, and a client that hung up mid-body got nothing instead of its refusal. Answers themselves did not change; when they are given did, on patient-side routes an anonymous visitor reaches.
Rejected: moving each of those checks into a dependency declared ahead of the body (a dozen new dependencies, and the `golden` action would still need its own way past the read); keeping the read in the dependency and only describing it (leaves the anonymous buffering).
Undo: revert the commit (the refusals then wait for the body again).
By: hospital agent
Source: `core/api_body.py` (`PendingBody`, `_for(reads=False)`, `deferred`, `lazy`), `admin_panel/admin_content_router.py`, `admin_panel/admin_patients_router.py`, `modules/intake/interview_patient_router.py`, `modules/turnos/booking_requests_router.py`; tests `test/routes/test_refusal_before_body.py` (every one of the 12 routes refuses with 64 bytes of a 100 000-byte body sent, then the client hangs up), `test/test_api_body.py` (`TestBodyInTheSignature.test_a_refusal_ahead_of_the_handler_read_never_reads_the_body`), `test/test_body_query_deps.py` (`LOADED_IN_THE_HANDLER`).

## 2026-11-11 · The staff cookie's compression rates are samples; the guarantee is only that a new cookie is plain
Decided: what the code guarantees since «A new staff cookie is never zlib-compressed» is one thing: every cookie the app signs is plain base64 JSON (`auth/admin_auth._PlainPayloadSerializer`). The rates and lengths in that entry («about 1 in 170» for the e2e desk account, «about 1 in 10» for a long doctor username, «1 to 21 characters longer») describe what the stock serializer did on one sample of 5000 payloads per account; they are not bounds. A second sample, reproducible (itsdangerous 2.2.0, Python 3.12.15, the real `_mint_token`, 5000 cookies per composition, 3 runs with a new random password stamp per account each run), measured: compressed every time for the owner from the environment (payload `v`, `u`, `r`, `exp`, `nonce`), for a username equal to its role with a stamp (`cs` added) and for every renewed cookie (`sid` added); for a fresh cookie of the e2e desk account (8-character username, `admisionista`) never, never, 1 in 85; of an 11-character doctor username 1 in 5000, never, 1 in 1667; of a 27-character one 55 %, 92 %, 29 %. Where the stock serializer compressed, the plain cookie was 1 to 23 characters longer; where its cookie was already plain, nothing changed. The docstring of `_PlainPayloadSerializer` now states the guarantee and calls the rates samples.
Why: the earlier entry and the docstring gave one sample's rates as facts; a second sample gives other rates for the same accounts, because the payload's password stamp is the account's and differs between accounts.
Undo: revert the commit (docstring only).
By: hospital agent
Source: `auth/admin_auth.py` (`_PlainPayloadSerializer` docstring).

## 2026-11-11 · The strict body boundary: a body that does not match its model is a 400 before the domain runs
- Until now the Pydantic model of a route's body only observed: a body that did not match was logged (`body_contract_drift`) and the domain answered it as it always had — its own text, its own status, and where it tolerated a wrong type (a number or a list where it reads text, an object where it reads a flag) it accepted the request. With `core.api_body.STRICT = True` such a body is answered exactly like a body that does not decode: at the same point of the route, 400 with the family's flat `{"error": "JSON inválido: campo <campo>: <qué se esperaba>"}` (or the family's own fixed text where it has one: «JSON inválido», «JSON inválido.»), with the family's headers, and the domain never sees it. One switch for every reader of `core/api_body.py` (`body`, `deferred`, `lazy`, `raw`, `lenient`, `read_model`, `checked`); no route was edited. On a `deferred()`/`lazy()` route the check runs where the handler reads the body (`await pending.read()`, and the domain's call of the `LazyBody` from `await pending.lazy()`), so a refusal ahead of that point still answers before a byte of the body arrives (`test/routes/test_refusal_before_body.py`, green with the switch on). A domain that reads its body itself (`LazyBody`: reschedule, waitlist, the referral analysis, assign, attendance) puts the same reason after its own «JSON inválido: ».
- Where it applies: the 72 routes whose body has a family contract. The routes without one answer every body themselves and keep only observing: the browser error reports, the table export, the «Recorrido» steps, the AI register notice, the usability study's four calls and the usability analysis and findings, the photo uploads, a chat turn (streamed or not) and the dictation.
- What changes for a client, pinned by the batteries (`test/golden/body_contract_*.json`): 818 of the 2381 answers on 68 of the 85 pinned routes; every other recorded answer (status, three headers, the normalized body) is the old one. Of the 818, 455 were already a 400 and only the text changes; 270 were a 2xx before (258 × 200, 12 × 204 of the two beacons, search log and the login panel's event, which now answer a JSON 400 instead of an empty 204); the rest were the domain's 404 (60: the portal's test identities and login switched off, the consult that does not exist), 409 (16), 401 (14: a wrong-type DNI or password on the patient's and the staff sign-in) and 429 (3). A sign-in body of the wrong type no longer counts as an attempt against the rate limit. Every client in the repository was read against the models (the panel, the patient site, the HSI extension, the scripts; the chat and MCP call the domain in process): no current client in the repository sends a body the boundary refuses.
- `test/routes/_body_contract.check_refusals` holds it on every pinned route with a contract: a case whose body decodes, by the family's own reading, to a value that does not match the model is the family's 400 (`refuses=False` only where the route answers before its body in the test, or has no contract). `test/test_api_body.TestStrictBoundary` holds it per reader, and that the switch off restores the observer. Five unit-test files pinned the domain's own 400 for a mismatched body and now get the family's 400 (their test apps install `api_body`'s handler, as the app does): `sobreturno: "si"` on the desk appointment (was «sobreturno_invalido»), `resultados: "no-numero"` on the search log (was «resultados inválido»), a `patient_reviewed`/`choices_reviewed` that is not an object on the consult confirm (was «Recargá…»; still nothing written), `records` that is not a list on a patient's clinical history, and `override: "si"` on the interview settings and a doctor's own. The response-model checks of the staff and the patient areas (`test/test_staff_response_docs.py`, `test/test_patient_response_docs.py`) check every pinned 2xx answer against its documented model and hold a floor on how many they check: those answers are now 49 and 80 (the rest became the family's 400), so the floors go from 90 and 100 to 45 and 75; every answer still checked is checked the same way.
Why: TASK-76 item F (the owner's decision 2026-11-11, «as in the textbook»): the models describe the API; now they also guard it.
Rejected: a 422 with FastAPI's `detail` list (a new error shape no client reads; the family's 400 is what a body that does not decode already gets); `body: Model` in the signature (TASK-76 item E's reasons); refusing on the routes without a contract (each has its own answers — the browser error reports must never be refused — and would need a contract of its own).
Undo: revert the commit (code, tests and golden files together). `STRICT = False` in `core/api_body.py` alone restores every old answer at runtime (the models observe again; the 500 → 400 of a mismatched body that crashes the domain stays), but the tests pin the strict answers: a hot fix, followed by the revert.
Source: `core/api_body.py`; tests `test/test_api_body.py`, `test/routes/_body_contract.py`, `test/routes/test_body_contract_*.py`, `test/test_admin_appointments.py`, `test/test_searches_router_native.py`, `test/test_intake_consult_admin.py`, `test/test_admin_patients.py`, `test/test_intake_settings.py`, `test/test_staff_response_docs.py`, `test/test_patient_response_docs.py`.
By: hospital agent

## 2026-11-12 · The day-queue event stream opens its LISTEN connection before the response starts; a database lost mid-stream ends it quietly
Decided: `GET /api/admin/day-queue/events` opens the stream's own LISTEN connection (`queue_events._Listener.open()`) in the route, before `StreamingResponse` is returned, and hands it to `queue_events.stream(listener, ...)`. A psycopg error on that connect (`ConnectionTimeout`, a refusal, any outage class `db._unavailable_error` recognises) becomes `db.DbConnectionLost`, so the app-level `DbUnavailable` handler answers the standard 503 `{"error": "base de datos no disponible"}` with `Retry-After: 3` and logs the reason; EventSource reconnects by itself. An error that is not an outage is not translated. If the LISTEN statement itself fails, the half-open connection is closed. Once the stream is running, a `DbUnavailable`-family error from the replay reads, or a psycopg `OperationalError` from the LISTEN connection, ends the stream with one warning line in `uvicorn.error` instead of an exception from inside the response; the browser reconnects with `Last-Event-ID` and replays from the durable `day_queue_events` rows. No UI change.
Why: Sentry TURNOS-SALUD-7 (release a97f8118, env salud, 2 events): the non-pooled LISTEN connection timed out after 5 s while the pooled database was healthy, and the error escaped from inside the StreamingResponse as an unhandled 500. That connection is outside the pool, so the pooled `with` never translated its failures.
Rejected: catching inside the generator only (the headers are already sent, so the client would see a 200 with an empty stream and no retry signal); a new error shape for the stream (the 503 is what every other route answers); retrying the connect in the route (the pool-side policy is to answer 503 and let the client retry).
Undo: revert the commit.
Source: `modules/turnos/queue_events.py` (`_Listener.open`, `stream`, `_lost_errors`), `modules/turnos/doctor_appointments_router.py` (`day_queue_events`); tests `test/test_queue_sse.py` (`QueueSseListenerFailureTest`), `test/test_doctor_appointments_router_native.py` (`test_queue_event_stream_whose_listen_connection_times_out_is_the_standard_503`).
By: agent hospital

## 2026-11-12 · Day-queue stream, review follow-up: only an outage ends it quietly, the first read is part of opening it, the response owns the close
Decided: four review notes of the entry above. (1) A running stream ends quietly only for an outage: `queue_events._is_outage` is true for the `DbUnavailable` family and for a psycopg error that `db._unavailable_error` classifies as one (SQLSTATE classes 08, 53, 57, 58, F0, no SQLSTATE, a statement or lock limit); a deadlock, a protocol violation (08P01) or any other error is raised as before, with the listener closed by the stream's `finally`. The same translation now covers a failing `LISTEN` statement in `_Listener.open()` (the half-open connection is closed first). (2) The cursor read that starts the feed (`day_queue_event_high_water`, none on a reconnect with `Last-Event-ID`) moved out of the generator into `queue_events.open_feed(last_event_id)`, which the route awaits before the response starts and which returns `(listener, cursor)`: a database that drops between the LISTEN connect and that read is the standard 503, and the listener is closed on that path. `stream(listener, cursor, identity, disconnected)` starts with `ready`. (3) The route passes `background=BackgroundTask(listener.close)` to the `StreamingResponse`: a generator that never starts never runs its `finally`, and the cancelled one of a client that hung up mid-stream is closed only when collected; the background task closes the LISTEN connection when the response ends however it ends. Closing twice is harmless. (4) `patient_web/site_base._now_art` reads `core.clock` (converted to the installation timezone as before), so `test_slot_green` freezes the clock with `test/_clock.py` instead of patching the function.
Why: the review of the merged fix: «deadlock / 08P01 logged as database unavailable», «the DB drops between open() and the first read and the stream ends as a 200 with an empty body», «a leaked LISTEN connection if the generator never starts», «patching `_now_art` directly when `core.clock` exists».
Rejected: a weakref finalizer for the leak (an async close cannot run from a finalizer); keeping the cursor read in the generator (the headers are sent by then, so an outage there cannot be a 503).
Undo: revert the commit.
Source: `modules/turnos/queue_events.py` (`open_feed`, `stream`, `_is_outage`, `_Listener.open`), `modules/turnos/doctor_appointments_router.py` (`day_queue_events`), `patient_web/site_base.py` (`_now_art`); tests `test/test_queue_sse.py` (`QueueSseListenerFailureTest`), `test/test_doctor_appointments_router_native.py`, `test/test_slot_green.py`.
By: agent hospital

## 2026-11-12 · Day-queue stream, second review follow-up: the response closes the LISTEN connection on every path, a failing close never replaces the first error
Decided: (1) The feed's response is `_FeedResponse(StreamingResponse)` in `modules/turnos/doctor_appointments_router.py`: its `__call__` closes the LISTEN connection in a `finally`, so the close happens whatever way the response ends — a client gone before the first byte (the body generator never starts and its own `finally` never runs), a failed send, a cancelled request. It replaces the `BackgroundTask` of the previous entry, which does not run when the send fails or the request is cancelled. (2) `queue_events.close_quietly(listener)` is the one way every path closes the connection (the stream's `finally`, the failed `LISTEN` in `_Listener.open()`, the failed cursor read in `open_feed()`, the response): shielded from cancellation (`anyio.CancelScope(shield=True)`), and a close that raises is logged by type (`log_pii.exc`) and swallowed, so it cannot turn the 503 of the first failure into a 500 nor hide an error the stream re-raises. Closing twice is harmless. (3) `site_availability._schedule_table_html` takes today's weekday from `site_base._now_art()` (so from `core.clock`) instead of `datetime.now`; `test_site_page_snapshots` no longer patches the `datetime` module of that file, the golden pages now hold the weekday through the clock alone. (4) Correction to the entry «The day-queue event stream opens its LISTEN connection before the response starts…»: its Source names `_lost_errors`, which no longer exists; it was replaced by `queue_events._is_outage` in the first follow-up (the mid-stream catch is `except Exception` that re-raises when `_is_outage` is false), and the stream's close is `close_quietly`.
Why: the second review of the merged follow-up: «the client drops between open_feed() and the start of the response and the background task never runs», «an exception while closing can replace the original LISTEN error: 500 instead of 503», «site_availability reads datetime.now directly», «the first entry names a function that is gone».
Rejected: a weakref finalizer (an async close cannot run from one); keeping the `BackgroundTask` next to the response's own close (a second path for the same close).
Undo: revert the commit.
Source: `modules/turnos/queue_events.py` (`close_quietly`, `_Listener.open`, `open_feed`, `stream`), `modules/turnos/doctor_appointments_router.py` (`_FeedResponse`), `patient_web/site_availability.py` (`_schedule_table_html`); tests `test/test_queue_sse.py` (`QueueSseListenerFailureTest`), `test/test_doctor_appointments_router_native.py` (`_feed_response`, `test_a_client_gone_before_the_first_byte_still_closes_the_listener`, `test_a_cancelled_response_closes_the_listener_to_the_end`), `test/test_site_page_snapshots.py`, `test/admin-cola.test.mjs`.
By: agent hospital

## 2026-11-12 · The application is one package, `app/`, and the tests live in `tests/`
Decided: every Python module of the application moves into one package, `app/` (the twelve packages unchanged inside it; `app.py` becomes `app/main.py`; `asgi_main.py`, `worker.py`, `manage.py` move in with three-line run-only shims left at the root until every installation starts with `python3 -m app`, `python3 -m app.worker`, `python3 -m app.manage`), and `test/` becomes `tests/`. The root keeps 17 directories and 26 files (`tests/test_repo_layout.py`); the policy documents go to `docs/policies/` and `.github/`, the files the site serves at `/` to `static/root/`, `robots.txt`/`llms.txt` to `templates/`, the design working files to `docs/design/`, `tools/` to `scripts/tools/`. `app/core/paths.py` is the one place that derives a directory from `__file__`. Public URLs, queue task names, environment keys, the database and the data volume do not change. The static fallback serves an explicit list (`/static/`, `/vendor/`, `/webdata/`, the root web assets, the assets module manifests declare): source, tests and documents answer 404 under any path, inside the asset trees too; a root web asset has its root URL only. The stray root copy `human-rounds-mark-padded.png` is deleted (nothing linked it; the working copy is in `static/`). The supervised queue worker is started by its file path, `app/worker.py`, not `-m app.worker`: a process table names it as before (the suite's worker-leak gate, an operator's `ps`); `python3 -m app.worker` starts the same worker by hand.
Why: bringing the code to the FastAPI standard before it is opened (TASK-76/77): the FastAPI documentation («Bigger Applications») and the official full-stack template put the application in one `app/` package with `app/main.py`, and `tests/`, `scripts/`, `alembic.ini` at the root. Before, a reader met 56 files and 30 directories at the root, application code mixed with tooling, and a `test/` that the standard library's `test` package shadows (`python -m unittest test.x` runs nothing of ours). The fallback served any checkout file with a served extension (the demo answered `/templates/about.html`).
Rejected: a `src/` root of domain packages (a community guide, not the documentation); `alembic/` inside `app/` as in the template (its frozen baseline and 42 revision files stay where they are — less risk); renaming domain packages (`cuentas` → `accounts`: the move stays one rule, every name keeps its last segment); moving the packages one at a time (a round of rewritten imports per package for every open branch instead of one); keeping the old import names alive through package shims (a stale `mock.patch("core.db.x")` would hit the shim and pass for the wrong reason — without them it fails loudly).
How it was made: by a program, not by hand — `scripts/layout/move.py` moves the files and rewrites what names them (imports, `mock.patch` targets, manifests, paths from the root in tests and scripts), one mechanical commit per stage (`Layout-Stage:` trailer), each followed by a small hand commit for what a program cannot decide; `scripts/layout/apply.py` replays the chain on a fresh `main` and gives the same trees. `scripts/layout/port_branch.py` carries an open branch across.
Undo: `git revert -m 1 <merge>` of the set's one `--no-ff` merge (no migration, no data change).
By: hospital agent
Source: `docs/LAYOUT.md`; `scripts/layout/`; `app/core/paths.py`; `app/services/static_assets.py` (`is_public_static`); tests `tests/test_repo_layout.py`, `tests/test_repo_paths.py`, `tests/test_static_allowlist.py`, `tests/test_root_shims.py`, `tests/test_queue_task_names.py`, `tests/test_layout_move.py`, `tests/test_layout_chain.py`, `tests/test_unittest_discover_guard.py`.

## 2026-11-12 · Each frontend test file runs in its own process with a deadline
Decided: `scripts/run_frontend_tests.mjs` no longer hands all files of a lane to one `node --test`. `runFiles()` starts one `node --test <file>` per file (the pure lane in parallel, the browser lane one at a time), kills the file's whole process group after `FRONTEND_FILE_TIMEOUT_MS` (default 5 minutes for a pure-JS file, 15 for a browser file; a killed file counts as one failed test in the summary) and fails the run with `TIMEOUT: <file>` in the log and in the failing-tests block at the end. The per-file summaries are merged into one `ℹ tests N` block per lane, so the test-count floor still reads the real total.
Why: `node --test --test-timeout` bounds single tests, not a file whose event loop stays alive after its tests passed; one such file hung the whole CI job instead of failing (finding of the hospital hub on main, 2026-11-12).
Rejected: only `--test-timeout` (does not cover a hung file, measured); a global timeout around the whole runner (kills the job without naming the file).
Undo: revert the commit.
Source: `scripts/run_frontend_tests.mjs` (`runFiles`, `FILE_TIMEOUT_MS`); tests `tests/frontend-lanes.test.mjs`.
By: agent hr-after-layout

## 2026-11-13 · `booking_decisions` asks `core.clock` for «now»; the day-queue test freezes it there
Decided: the four places in `app/modules/turnos/booking_decisions.py` that read the machine's clock (`datetime.now(timezone.utc)` for the birth-date check, the «Ese turno ya pasó» checks of cancel/reschedule, and `datetime.now(bkr._ART)` for the same-day rule of «Reprogramar») now call `core.clock` (`utcnow()` for the instants and the birth date, `today()` for the installation's calendar day); the behaviour is the same. `test_the_day_queue` in `tests/routes/test_staff_response_models.py` pins the application's «now» to 08:00 today with `tests/_clock.py` (`freeze`) instead of `patch.object(booking_decisions, "datetime", ...)`. The guard `tests/test_booking_cut.py::TestPatchesAimAtTheOwner` is unchanged: it refuses a patch of an imported name, and the module no longer needs one.
Why: main 755672cb7 was red on exactly that guard — the test replaced the imported name `datetime` in `booking_decisions` because the module read the machine's clock itself, which is the seam `core.clock` exists to replace.
Rejected: relaxing the guard to allow the patch (it pins that a patch aims at something the module defines); patching `datetime` in the module differently (the same imported-name patch).
Undo: revert the commit.
Source: `app/modules/turnos/booking_decisions.py`, `app/core/clock.py`; tests `tests/test_booking_cut.py`, `tests/routes/test_staff_response_models.py` (`test_the_day_queue`).

## 2026-11-13 · Public tables: «No hay turnos» stacks its date, text columns wrap, the 901–1120 grid has as many tracks as columns
Decided: in the public listing engine (`static/platform/listing.mjs`), the closed-agenda cell writes «No hay turnos disponibles» and «Se habilitan a partir de: …» as two lines (`.lst-closed-when` is a block, the « · » separator is gone) that may wrap, and the open note may wrap too; at 901 px and wider the text, link and venue cells wrap with `text-wrap:balance`. In the 901–1120 band, where a doctors' table becomes one grid per row, the row template has as many tracks as the table has visible columns (no Centro on a center's page, no Centro and no Orden on a study's «Quién atiende», a uniform column collapsed into a note): `tableHtml` writes it into `--lst-av-grid` on the table from the columns it renders, one track width per column kind (`AV_TRACK`), and both CSS copies read that variable; the cells after the name take the next tracks in order, a row grows with its tallest cell (`min-height:56px` instead of a cell height that capped it), and a name that wraps above its specialty starts at the avatar (`justify-content:flex-start` wherever the name and specialty sit in a row, phone included). `static/platform/chat-widget.mjs` mirrors the band as a container query, and `#stzc-page` is always a container, so the mirror carries the same rules.
Why: the product owner's frames of salud.pinamar.gob.ar/?q=medici (2026-11-13): «Atención» of Telemedicina ran to three lines and «Servicio» to two while the long «No hay turnos disponibles · Se habilitan a partir de: Domingo 15 de noviembre, 08:00» stayed on one line — a `nowrap` cell's min-content equals its max-content, so the table gave it all the width it asked for. On live pinamar data the 1024 doctors' grid also left an empty track in the middle of a center's page and spilled a wrapped «Más horarios» onto the next row, and a phone row without a button centred the doctor's name.
Rejected: a max width on the name columns (decided against earlier: names have no cap); shortening the opening text (it is data the hospital reads); hiding the venue column on a center's page when only one service has a venue (a closed product decision of 2026-07-11).
Undo: revert the commit.
Source: `static/platform/listing.mjs` (`habilitaCell`, the ≥901 and 901–1120 rules), `static/platform/chat-widget.mjs` (the 901–1120 and ≤900 container mirrors); tests `tests/listing-order.test.mjs`, `tests/e2e/listing-long-name-fit.spec.mjs`.
By: agent hr-table-fit

## 2026-11-13 · Reports: «Donde más se pierde» counts only real losses; the hours advice needs 20 requests; insurers merge by case
Decided: (1) In Recorrido/Gestión the biggest loss is chosen by the step's `perdidos` (rejected, cancelled, said no, did not come), not by the difference between steps; requests still on their way (`pendiente`, `por_venir`) get a hero line of their own («Aparte: N siguen en curso…»). (2) `sin_marcar` is no loss: its line says «Asistencia sin marcar: N» and links to Pacientes de hoy. (3) A pending request older than 30 days has its own reason `colgada` (`db_reports.reports_patient_path` adds the flag), also not a loss. (4) The extra-hours advice (`_hours_suggestion`, the hero's investment sentence) needs `MIN_SOLICITUDES_SUGERENCIA` = 20 requests in the period. (5) The Gestión tile says «Aprobadas ahora, de las resueltas» and the funnel adds «Les dieron turno / Siguen aprobadas / aprobadas y canceladas después»; a longer median wait is tone `atencion`. (6) Insurers are grouped by case-folded, space-collapsed name and show the most frequent spelling; an account naming one twice counts once.
Why: the analytics review of 13.11 found the hero blaming «17, 63 %» on pinamar of which 4 were turnos ahead and «130, 87 %» on the demo of which 129 were ahead; advice to add hours rested on a handful of requests; «IOMA»/«ioma» were two rows.
Rejected: keeping the whole-difference rule (it presents pending work as lost); hiding the advice altogether (it is right at volume).
Undo: revert the commit.

## 2026-11-13 · The inbound HSI sync links a turno the desk loaded by hand with the extension
Decided: `hsi_journal._reconcile_appointment`, before it imports an HSI appointment that no request links, looks for exactly one approved request without `hsi_appointment_id` of the same patient (the observation's DNI, or the `hsi_patients` mirror's when the observation carries none — and a DNI-less observation, an appointment listing's patient stub, no longer overwrites the DNI or the record the patient poll learned in `db_hsi.hsi_mirror_patient`) and the same minute (`db_hsi.hsi_unlinked_approved_at`, DNI+minute first so a foreign appointment never pays the catalog scan), narrowed to the same catalog doctor (the diary's `professional_name` resolved by `doctor_appointments.resolve_doctor_id`, as `hsi_busy` does). One → `db_hsi.hsi_link_request` sets `hsi_appointment_id`, `hsi_patient_id` (only when still empty) and `hsi_status` «ok» under the row lock — re-asserting the DNI, doctor and minute there, so a request the desk moved between the search and the lock is refused — with an `appointment_events` row of kind `hsi_vinculada`, and the normal branch follows (attendance with origin `hsi`, cancellation as a conflict, move). Several → a conflict on each candidate, no link. None → `imported` as before. Never linked: a request already linked, a cancelled or blocked HSI appointment, a time without an offset, an observation without a DNI, a request whose own appointment intent in `hsi_outbox` is still in flight — pending, sending, error (that one is a conflict: linking it could leave the journal opening the same turno twice); an intent HSI refused for good (`conflict`) does not block, that refusal is why the desk loads it by hand, and a later replay of that create skips a linked request. Nothing is written to HSI. Two guards keep the journal the ONE driver: the two new conflict reasons are a `vinculo` cluster of the HSI resolver (a title and a remedy, closed once the request is linked — for the in-flight case, linked to that very HSI turno — or no longer approved; a conflict already open with the same reason is not opened again), and `GET /api/admin/day-queue/changes` answers without `turno` for a request the journal links while an EMR connector is routed, so the extension's panel never offers to replay in HSI what the server already did (`doctor_appointments._server_drives`).
Why: turnos loaded into HSI through «HSI Humanizador» fill HSI's own screen and never learn the HSI id, so attendance, cancellation and moves made in HSI never came back to them (task of the pinamar-turnos hub, 2026-11-13). Variant A: on the server, no new extension version.
Attendance reality (asked of the local HSI stand `historiaclinica/hsi:v2.71.2`, its own Postgres): HSI has no separate attendance field — attendance IS `appointment_state_id`, whose catalog is 1 Asignado, 2 Confirmado, 3 Ausente, 4 Cancelado, 5 Atendido, 6 Reserva, 7 Bloqueo, 8 Fuera, 9 Reserva ausente. The connector already maps 3/9 → ausente and 5 → presente, so a state HSI records DOES come back through the link. But HSI's turnos screen disables «Ausente» and «Atendido» for everyone (the desk's only enabled mark is «Confirmado» = state 2 = `scheduled`), so in practice the link returns cancellation and moves; real attendance arrives only if HSI's clinical side ever sets 5/3/9 on its own.
Retry: an HSI appointment observed again unchanged is retried while it is unlinked, not cancelled or blocked and from today on (`hsi_journal._relink_unchanged`), so a request becomes linked once it turns linkable after the first sighting — the patient poll learned the DNI, the desk approved the request or set its doctor, the extra candidate of a conflict was rejected — and an appointment imported before this change links on the next tick. Cost: up to three indexed reads per such appointment per tick, bounded by the agendas from today on.
Limits: it works only where the inbound sync runs (an EMR connector selected, `TURNOS_SKIP_HSI` off). A request whose doctor is stored by name only (`medico_asignado_id` empty) never matches: without the catalog id the doctor is not proven, and the turno stays with the extension as before.
Rejected: the extension reading the id back from HSI's screen and posting it (a new extension version and a store review); matching without the doctor (two doctors at the same minute for one patient would be guessed); relinking an already linked request (the id the push recorded wins); a unique index on `hsi_appointment_id` (the duplicate case is already a visible conflict, and the row lock serializes the one-candidate link).
Undo: revert the commit (no migration; `hsi_vinculada` rows stay as history).
Source: `app/modules/turnos/hsi_journal.py` (`_link_unlinked_request`, `_relink_unchanged`), `app/core/db_hsi.py` (`hsi_diary_professional`, `hsi_mirror_patient_dni`, `hsi_unlinked_approved_at`, `hsi_link_request`, `hsi_conflict_open`, `HSI_LINKED`), `app/modules/turnos/doctor_appointments.py` (`_server_drives`), `app/connectors/hsi/hsi_resolver.py` (`vinculo`), `docs/architecture/hsi.md`; tests `tests/test_hsi_extension_link.py`, `tests/test_cola_dia.py`, `tests/test_hsi_resolver.py`, `tests/test_db_hsi_store.py`.
By: agent hr-hsi-link

## 2026-11-13 · The patient-summary sweep is off by default on the demo

Decided: `chart_summary._pregen_enabled()` (the one truth read by `start()` and the worker registry) now answers no on a demo installation (`TURNOS_DEMO_MODE`) when `TURNOS_SUMMARY_PREGEN` is unset; `TURNOS_SUMMARY_PREGEN=1` brings the sweep back, `0` still turns it off everywhere. On the demo the summary is made when a doctor opens it, as before.

An unchanged patient was already not recomputed; the data itself changes nightly.

## 2026-11-13 · Review of the HSI link and the funnel report: five fixes
Decided: (1) the journal's retry of an unchanged HSI appointment (`_relink_unchanged`) first looks its minute up in a `_RelinkPool` read once per tick (`db_hsi.hsi_unlinked_approved_minutes`, one query of the approved unlinked requests from today on): a minute with no such request costs no query, instead of three on separate connections per hospital turno under the exclusive lock; (2) `hsi_mirror_patient` with no DNI on a row that has none replaces the payload only with a record that adds something (the stored payload does not contain it), so an appointment listing's stub never swaps the full record every tick; (3) `day_changes_data` leaves an access-log row for every request it answers about, including the ones the server drives (`estado`/`oculto` without `turno`); (4) `_linkable_instant` is the one cancelled/blocked/time check of `_relink_unchanged` and `_link_unlinked_request`; (5) `reports_funnel._accion` raises when a phrase that counts (`{n}`) gets no `n`, and the 30-day «colgada» threshold is `db_reports.COLGADA_DIAS`, used by the SQL interval and as `{dias}` in the phrase and the ten locales; (6) `_server_drives` answers nobody while `TURNOS_HSI_SYNC_OFF=1` (`hsi_journal._off()`), so the extension keeps the turno when nothing else drives it; (7) `hsi_mirror_patient` turns an empty/blank DNI into no data, never erasing the stored one.
Why: the code review of the extension-link and report changes found each of them; every one was reproduced by a test first.
Rejected: a short horizon for the relink (a request approved for a later day would never link); hiding `estado` of server-driven requests instead of logging them (the extension needs it).
Undo: revert the commit; no schema change.
Source: `app/modules/turnos/hsi_journal.py`, `app/core/db_hsi.py`, `app/modules/turnos/doctor_appointments.py`, `app/admin_panel/reports_funnel.py`, `app/core/db_reports.py`, `locales/*/admin.srv.json`; tests `tests/test_hsi_extension_link.py`, `tests/test_cola_dia.py`, `tests/test_reports_funnel.py`.

## 2026-11-13 · El sitio ofrece cada dia completo, hasta la ultima hora de la agenda
Decided: the patient grid no longer cuts a day at 12 hours. `site_availability._agenda_slots`, `_appointments_for_service` and `_appointments_for_doctor` keep every hour of each of the first six days (`_SLOTS_DAYS` stays); `_SLOTS_PER_DAY` and `_slots_per_day()` are deleted.
Why: A doctor (Diabetologia, CAPS Valeria del Mar) has HSI hours 13:00-20:00 every 20 min and the patient saw only 13:00-16:40 (12 chips); the cut ran in `_agenda_slots` BEFORE busy hours were removed, so a day with two hours taken showed 10 chips and still nothing after 17:00. Reported by the hospital counterpart 2026-11-13, twice. Not a timezone error: the 3 h gap is 12 chips x 20 min against a 7 h window.
Rejected: raising the cap to 48 (a 14 h agenda at 15 min is 56); capping after removing busy hours (still hides the evening of a long agenda). Chips wrap, the grid has no fixed height.
Cost: a long agenda shows up to ~40 chips a day; none measured on the phone.
Undo: revert the commit; no schema change.

## 2026-11-13 · Security audit of the patient MCP connector: lifetime, revoke, password change, unbound identity
Decided: audit of `/mcp` and `/api/account/mcp*` pinned by `tests/test_mcp_security.py`. Fixes: (1) a password change clears the MCP token (`set_password`); (2) `POST /api/account/mcp/regenerate` and the new `/revoke` go through the Origin check of `admin_csrf_middleware` (`_PATIENT_PATHS`); (3) `tools/list` offers only tools `mcp_guard` can actually serve; (4) the owner's security decision (pinamar-turnos, 2026-11-13): the token dies after 30 days WITHOUT USE (sliding window: `users.mcp_token_at`, `users.mcp_token_used` written at most every 10 minutes, alembic 0043; existing tokens start their clock on the migration day), plus a password change, a portal identity that stops matching its account (`accounts.current_user` revokes the token when the DNI of the portal account differs from the CUIT's), an erased account (`personal_data`) and an explicit revoke (`POST /api/account/mcp/revoke`, «Desconectar el asistente»); logging out of the browser does NOT touch the token (a connected app of its own); «Mi cuenta» shows the last use. An expired token is replaced by the next visit to «Mi cuenta».
Why: the token is a bearer credential held by a third-party assistant, and the portal never tells us about an unbinding of a CUIT, so the lifetime is the bound on how long an unbound person's old token can live.
Rejected: a fixed lifetime from issue (cuts working assistants off); killing the token on browser logout; asking the portal for unbinding events (it does not send any).
Undo: revert the commit; the two columns are nullable and unused by older code.
Source: `app/core/db_accounts.py`, `app/modules/chat/mcp_api.py`, `app/modules/chat/mcp_api_router.py`, `app/core/admin_csrf_middleware.py`, `app/cuentas/accounts.py`, `alembic/versions/0043_mcp_token_lifetime.py`, `static/platform/account.mjs`; tests `tests/test_mcp_security.py`.

## 2026-11-13 · «Resuelta por» se escribe como frase, con un solo formateador
Decided: `solicitudes.resuelta_por` (`auto:web`, `mesa:<usuario>`, `medico:<usuario>`) ya no se muestra crudo. `format.resolved_by_text` / `format.mjs:resueltaPor` (paridad en `tests/formato.test.mjs`) escribe «Automática: el paciente sacó el turno en la página» (columna: «Automática (web)»), «Mesa de entrada · <usuario>» y «Médico · <nombre del médico>»; un prefijo desconocido se muestra sin prefijo y vacío no muestra nada. Rejected: migrar los valores guardados (es auditoría); inventar un nombre de operador.
Cost: un valor sin prefijo (aprobado desde la cola con solo el usuario) se muestra tal cual.
